# Tasks — TangramPlaytime

**Status:** Active  ·  **Last updated:** 2026-10-21

> Each task links back to the requirement(s) it implements via REQ IDs and
> the acceptance IDs it covers, so the traceability chain
> `REQ → task → code → test` stays intact. Do not invent tasks for work no
> requirement asked for (no silent scope). Columns and rules:
> `C:/GitHub/AI/SWDev/framework/templates/tasks.md` — **Inputs** (files
> outside the module its tests read) and **Done-check** (the test that must
> be green at that step) are mandatory; a **Status** cell is one word plus
> a link to the WO log, edited with the Edit tool only.

Two lanes. The test lane is TASK-003 [P] (Acceptance Test Author). The implementer lane is TASK-001 → TASK-002, hardest first. Rows are in landing order. The kit lands in two stages: stage 1 beside TASK-001, and stage 2 once TASK-001 is done, because Gradle compiles the whole `kernel/src/test` set at once. Briefs: `workorders/WO-001-briefs.md`. Verification channels (G3): JVM `:kernel:test` and `:contracts:test` only; no device channel.

## WO-002 — #Content: the puzzle library in :content behind IPuzzleLibrary  (Mode: Full (b))

There are two lanes:
- **Test lane:** TASK-008 (Acceptance Test Author). It drafts the kit in its scratchpad while TASK-004 runs, and lands it once TASK-004 is green.
- **Implementer lane:** TASK-004 → TASK-005 → TASK-007. The hardest task (the reading path) goes first.

Rows are in landing order. Briefs: `workorders/WO-002-briefs.md`.

- **Kit landing.** The kit is drafted beside TASK-004 and lands in **one stage**, once TASK-004's Done-check is green, because Gradle compiles the whole `content/src/test` set at once. The parallel drafting is dispatch, not a plan dependency: TASK-008 depends on TASK-004, and TASK-005 depends on TASK-008.
- **Red window.** From the kit's landing until TASK-005 lands, `:content:testDebugUnitTest` does not compile. The only errors are unresolved references to TASK-005's seams. TASK-008's Done-check is therefore met at landing, and the green verdict on the kit is TASK-005's Done-check.
- **No TASK-006.** The design's suggested geometry task is merged into TASK-005 (design review F2), so that id is not used.
- **No task is [P].** All four tasks declare the read-only input `Tangrams/*.json`, and the v0.18 rule serializes tasks that share a declared input. TASK-008 also depends on TASK-004's reader. TASK-007 (tools) shares no file with the Kotlin lane but reads the same puzzle files, so it runs last in the implementer lane.
- **Inputs.** The bundled puzzles count as an input of every content task. The tests read build-time copies, but the bytes come from `Tangrams/*.json`.
- **Verification channels (G3):**
  - JVM `:content:testDebugUnitTest` and `:contracts:test`;
  - the Gradle artifact listing (TASK-004, review F3);
  - the Python tool channel (TASK-007);
  - no device channel.
- **Not covered here** (no token; WO § Scope notes):
  - REQ-038 A2: the pending deletion CHG, carried `implemented (unverified)`;
  - REQ-039 A1 and A2: WO-003 and WO-009;
  - REQ-042 A1: the content batch WO and WO-009;
  - REQ-045 A2: WO-003;
  - the `:app` wiring of `PuzzleLibraries.bundled`: WO-003 (review F4).

## WO-003 — #Solving: the Play screen in :play (board, tray, gestures, preview, solved picture)  (Mode: Light)

Mode **Light** (`workorders/WO-003.md` § Mode; decisions.md line 55). There is no design, so the planner writes the seams a UI kit needs in § Seams below: the kit authors and the implementers build against the same names without reading each other. Briefs: `workorders/WO-003-briefs.md`.

- **Lanes.**
  - **Test lane:** TASK-009 (the JVM kit, Opus) lands first. TASK-011 (the device kit, Fable) drafts beside TASK-009 and TASK-010 and lands once TASK-010 is green.
  - **Implementer lane:** TASK-010 → TASK-012 → TASK-013 → TASK-014 → TASK-015. Hardest first: the state holder and the gesture logic, then the rendering core, then every touch, then the decorations and the solve; the mechanical `:app` wiring goes last.
  - Rows are in landing order. The authors' parallel drafting is dispatch, not a plan dependency.
- **Task class** (staffing, WO § Constraints). Each Description starts with it. Fable: TASK-010, TASK-011, TASK-012, TASK-013, TASK-014. Opus: TASK-009 (a pure-JVM kit) and TASK-015 (mechanical wiring, the verifier and the reset script).
- **Three test source sets, each compiled at once by Gradle — the red windows:**
  - `play/src/test` (JVM). From TASK-009's landing until TASK-010 lands, the set does not compile. The only errors are unresolved references to S-1…S-9 and to `LockEngine` / `PuzzleLibraries` (TASK-010 adds `:kernel` and `:content` as test dependencies). `PlayScaffoldTest` cannot run in that window. The green verdict is TASK-010's Done-check.
  - `play/src/androidTest` (device). From TASK-011's landing until TASK-012 lands, the set does not compile (S-10…S-14 and the androidTest dependencies TASK-012 adds). From TASK-012 on it compiles, and the classes of TASK-013 and TASK-014 fail at run time until each lands. Green verdicts: TASK-012 (BoardTrayKitTest), TASK-013 (DragKitTest, PreviewKitTest), TASK-014 (the other three, then the whole set on both images).
  - `app/src/androidTest` (device). From TASK-011's landing until TASK-012 lands, the set does not compile (the launch test reads S-13/S-14 of `:play`), so `FloorChannelTest` cannot run either. From TASK-012 until TASK-015, `AppLaunchKitTest` fails at run time (the skeleton shows only the app name). The green verdict is TASK-015's Done-check.
  - Nobody runs a set for a verdict inside its window.
- **No task is [P].** Every task's tests read the bundled puzzles `Tangrams/*.json` through `:content` (a shared declared input, `task-decomposition.md` v0.5), and each kit must land between two implementer steps.
- **Lines covered twice, on purpose.**
  - REQ-045.A1: BoardTrayKitTest (a fresh `PlayController`, under `play/`) satisfies trace-check's code-home rule for `#PuzzleMini` → `play/` (build-map v1.3; decisions.md line 57). AppLaunchKitTest is the real fresh start: the real wiring and the bundle read on the device (WO-002 code review N4).
  - REQ-020.A1: on the JVM (TASK-009) and on the device (TASK-011), both under `play/` (`#GoHome` → `play/`). REQ-017.A1–A2: the step counting on the JVM and the two-finger gesture on the device. The kernel and content tests keep their tokens as extra coverage.
- **Unverified by design:** REQ-002.A1 (TASK-010) and REQ-014.A1 (TASK-013) go green and stay `implemented (unverified)` for `capture-outbox/chg-draft-req002-a1-mirror.md` and `capture-outbox/chg-draft-req014-a1-without-delay.md` (decisions.md line 58).
- **Verification channels (G3):** JVM `:play:testDebugUnitTest` + `:contracts:test`; the dev image `Medium_Phone_API_37.0`; the floor image `Phone_API_26` (TASK-012 runs the no-anti-aliasing-seam proof there, TASK-014 the whole play device set, Test & Verify every set at close); the release verifier with its positive control (TASK-015 and close). One emulator at a time; `tools/device_reset.sh` before and after each device step once TASK-015 has landed it. Human eyes are not a DoD channel. The commands are in the briefs' channel table.
- **Not covered here** (no token):
  - REQ-002.A2 → WO-009 (decisions.md line 56); REQ-039.A2 → WO-009 (decisions.md line 40).
  - REQ-019 and REQ-022 are the kernel's (WO-001): consumed here, no new token.
  - REQ-037.A1, REQ-035 and REQ-047.A1–A2 are WO-006's. The screen follows their rules (the 60 dp badge area, the title language) without a token.
  - The three routing drafts (REQ-017 second finger, REQ-018 badge precedence, REQ-020 interrupted drag), the TYPE-006 transitions and the O-07 events are pinned untokened (decisions.md line 59).
  - Persistence, the top bar's ‹ › / counter / Restart and the solved bar (WO-004); sounds and haptics (WO-007); the tablet layout and insets (WO-006).

### Seams (the planner's, in place of a design seam table)

Package `app.tangramplaytime.play` and folder `play/src/main/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/play/` unless a row says otherwise. A seam is public where `:app` or `app/src/androidTest` needs it and `internal` otherwise (the test source sets of `:play` see `internal`). The kits call these names: a seam that must change during P4 is a `recirculate → planner`, never a quiet edit.

| # | Seam (file) | Shape | Called by | Delivered by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-1 | `PlayController` — construction and reads (`PlayController.kt`) | Public `class PlayController(library: IPuzzleLibrary, engine: ILockEngine, onEvent: (PlayEvent) -> Unit = {})`. Construction opens `library.puzzles.first()`: state New, nothing placed, every piece in the tray at its S-9 resting turn, not mirrored (the fresh start; WO-004 later opens the last-opened puzzle).<br>Reads are Compose snapshot state, readable from a JVM test without a composition: `puzzle: PuzzleDefinition` · `state: PuzzleState` (TYPE-006) · `placed: List<Placement>` (lock order; every entry a `LockResult.Locked` placement) · `trayPieces: Set<PieceId>` (the puzzle's pieces neither placed nor dragged, tray order) · `trayTurn(piece): Turn` · `trayMirrored(piece): Boolean` · `dragged: Dragged?` · `preview: Placement?` · `pulse: Pulse?` · `shake: Shake?` · `internal val engine: ILockEngine` (the screen draws with `vertices`) | both kits, S-10, S-15 | TASK-010 |
| S-2 | `PlayController` — actions | No time and no animation inside (the screen animates); main thread only.<br>`open(id: String): Boolean` — a listed puzzle (false and no change for an unknown id, G-15). A running drag ends as `goHome()` first. State and placements are kept in memory per puzzle id across opens (a Solved puzzle keeps its final placements; WO-004 swaps the memory for `IProgressStore`). Tray turns and mirror reset to S-9 on every open (decisions.md line 29).<br>`var boardDpPerUnit: Double` — passed to every engine search; the screen sets it at each layout, a JVM test sets it. A search while it is not > 0 throws `IllegalStateException` (a programming error, G-15).<br>`tapTray(piece)` — REQ-016: `Turn.next()` of a tray piece; no TYPE-006 change; `Turned`.<br>`tapBoard(piece): Boolean` — REQ-016: `tryTurnInPlace(…, current.turn.next(), …)`. `Locked` → the placement is replaced, `Turned`, true. `None` → unchanged, `shake` for it, false.<br>`pressBadge(): Boolean` — REQ-018: PG in the tray → `trayMirrored(PG)` toggles, `Turned`, true. PG placed → `tryMirrorInPlace`: `Locked` → replaced, `Turned`, true; `None` → unchanged, `shake`, false. No PG in the puzzle, PG dragged, or state Solved → false, no change.<br>`startDrag(piece, centre: Point): Boolean` — the piece leaves the tray (with its tray turn and mirror) or the board (with its placement's turn and mirror; it leaves `placed`) and becomes `dragged` at `centre` (puzzle units). False and no change when it is not the puzzle's piece, a drag is running, or the state is Solved. A start from the tray moves New → In progress (TYPE-006). `PickedUp`.<br>`moveDrag(centre: Point, overBoard: Boolean)` — stores the dragged centre and whether a release now would be over the board; no search.<br>`twistDrag(steps: Int)` — REQ-017: `steps` 45° steps of the dragged piece, positive = clockwise (`next` / `previous`); one `Turned` per step.<br>`updatePreview()` — REQ-021: `preview` = the `findLock` placement for the dragged piece at its centre, turn and mirror, or null (`None`, `overBoard` false, no drag). The screen calls it at most once per displayed frame.<br>`drop(): Placement?` — the player's release. The lock is the `updatePreview()` result for the current centre (computed now if not yet), so a release locks where the preview is (REQ-021 A1). `Locked` → appended, `Locked`, returned; then `isSolved` → state Solved and `Solved`. Otherwise (including `overBoard` false) the piece goes back to its cell (REQ-020), `WentHome`, `pulse` set in a mini puzzle only (REQ-051), null.<br>`goHome()` — a drag ended without a release (pointer cancel, the activity stopping; decisions.md line 59): as a missed drop but without `pulse`; a no-op without a drag | both kits, S-10, S-15 | TASK-010 |
| S-3 | Value types (`PlayController.kt`) | Public `data class Dragged(val piece: PieceId, val turn: Turn, val mirrored: Boolean, val centre: Point, val fromTray: Boolean, val overBoard: Boolean)` · `data class Pulse(val corners: List<Point>, val serial: Int)` — corners = `engine.outlineCorners(puzzle)`, a new serial per pulse · `data class Shake(val piece: PieceId, val serial: Int)` — a new serial per refused turn or mirror | both kits, S-10 | TASK-010 |
| S-4 | `PlayEvent` (`PlayEvent.kt`) | Public `sealed interface PlayEvent` (O-07): `PickedUp(piece)`, `Turned(piece)`, `Locked(piece)`, `WentHome(piece)`, `Solved(puzzleId: String)`, emitted synchronously through `onEvent` by the S-2 actions as listed there. `:play` plays nothing (WO-007) | JVM kit (pins), S-15 | TASK-010 |
| S-5 | `PlayLayout` (`PlayLayout.kt`) | Public `data class PlayLayout(val trayRows: List<List<PieceId>>, val floatOffsetDp: Float, val cellGapDp: Float, val sideMarginDp: Float, val topBarDp: Float)` — `:app` decides it (O-05).<br>**The phone value**, which the kits write as a literal and `:app` as its constant (S-15): rows `[[LT1, LT2, MT], [SQ, PG, ST1, ST2]]`, float offset 30 (REQ-014 ASSUMPTION), gap 8, side margin 8, top bar 66 (DI-2 §2–§3; the decisions row is TASK-010's). No tablet value (WO-006) | both kits, S-6, S-10, S-15 | TASK-010 |
| S-6 | `TrayMetrics` (`TrayMetrics.kt`) | Internal `object TrayMetrics { fun layout(widthDp: Float, heightDp: Float, layout: PlayLayout, pieces: Set<PieceId>): TrayGeometry }`; `data class TrayGeometry(val scaleDpPerUnit: Float, val heightDp: Float, val cells: Map<PieceId, CellRect>)`; `data class CellRect(val xDp: Float, val yDp: Float, val widthDp: Float, val heightDp: Float)` (tray-local dp).<br>REQ-013's rules: the scale comes from the full set's rows whatever `pieces` holds; width = turn diameter × scale + 12 dp; cells are square; `cells` holds exactly `pieces`, in the layout's rows with empty rows dropped (REQ-012 rule). `widthDp` / `heightDp` are the screen composable's size | JVM kit (REQ-013.A1), S-10 | TASK-010 |
| S-7 | `TwistTracker` (`TwistTracker.kt`) | Internal `class TwistTracker(startAngleDeg: Double) { fun steps(angleDeg: Double): Int }` — the net 45° steps for the second finger now at `angleDeg` around the first (the screen angle, y down, so positive = clockwise), unwrapped across ±180°. Boundaries at ±22.5°, ±67.5° … from the start (REQ-017): 20° → 0, 90° → 2, −90° → −2. A twist exactly on a boundary is TASK-010's recorded decision | JVM kit (REQ-017), TASK-013 | TASK-010 |

The touch is a drag from the move at which it has moved ≥ the slop from its down point (true from then on). There is no time input, so a touch that ends without becoming a drag is a tap whatever its duration (REQ-015) | JVM kit (pins), TASK-013 | TASK-010 |

**Tag table** (S-13 names, S-14 keys). A node's presence follows the controller at once. Its bounds, `CentreKey` and `DrawnPxPerUnitKey` follow the drawing in each frame (the grow, the glides).

| Tag (`PlayTags`) | Node | Present while | Bounds | Keys |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `play-screen` (`SCREEN`) | the screen root; every test touch is injected here | always | the screen | `PuzzleIdKey`, `PuzzleStateKey` |
| `title` (`TITLE`) | the puzzle title; merged, inert | always | the text | its text |
| `board` (`BOARD`) | the board area | always | the board | `BoardMappingKey` |
| `silhouette` (`SILHOUETTE`) | the flat silhouette | always | the silhouette's bounding box | — |
| `tray` (`TRAY`) | the tray strip | always | the strip | — |
| `tray-cell-<ID>` (`trayCell(id)`) | one cell per piece of the puzzle | the puzzle is unsolved | the S-6 cell | `PieceKey` |
| `tray-piece-<ID>` (`trayPiece(id)`) | the miniature | the piece is in `trayPieces` | the drawn shape's box | `PieceKey`, `TurnKey`, `MirroredKey`, `DrawnPxPerUnitKey` |
| `tray-ghost-<ID>` (`trayGhost(id)`) | the dashed outline in the cell | the piece is placed or dragged | the outline's box | `PieceKey` |
| `size-mark-<ID>` (`sizeMark(id)`) | S, M or L; merged, inert | a triangle is in the tray | the mark | its text |
| `flip-badge-tray` (`FLIP_BADGE_TRAY`) | the badge at the PG cell | PG is in the tray | the 60 dp touch area | — |
| `flip-badge-board` (`FLIP_BADGE_BOARD`) | the badge next to the placed PG | PG is placed and the puzzle is unsolved | the 60 dp touch area | — |
| `board-piece-<ID>` (`boardPiece(id)`) | a locked piece | the piece is placed, until the picture is fully shown | the drawn shape's box | `PieceKey`, `TurnKey`, `MirroredKey`, `CentreKey`, `DrawnPxPerUnitKey` |
| `drag-piece-<ID>` (`dragPiece(id)`) | the dragged piece | the piece is dragged | the drawn shape's box | the same five |
| `landing-preview` (`LANDING_PREVIEW`) | the dashed preview | `preview` is not null | the outline's box | `PieceKey`, `TurnKey`, `MirroredKey`, `CentreKey` |
| `corner-pulse` (`CORNER_PULSE`) | the pulse layer | about 600 ms per `Pulse` (also with reduced motion, unanimated) | the board | — |
| `corner-marker` (`CORNER_MARKER`) | one per outline corner, inside the pulse | with the pulse | centred on its corner | `CentreKey` |
| `confetti` (`CONFETTI`) | the confetti | during the celebration; never with reduced motion | — | — |
| `solved-picture` (`SOLVED_PICTURE`) | the picture | from the fade's start (600 ms after the last lock) | the silhouette's box | — |

**Touch routing** (TASK-013; the badge branch is TASK-014's). Pinned untokened, decisions.md line 59.
1. While a drag runs, every further touch is a twist finger for that drag (S-7 → `twistDrag`), whatever it lands on. Lifting it keeps the turn reached.
2. Otherwise a new touch belongs to the first hit of: a badge's 60 dp area → a tray cell (the whole cell, REQ-015) → a placed piece's shape grown by 12 dp (REQ-015; inside a shape beats another piece's margin; the last-locked piece is on top) → nothing. *Superseded 2026-10-12 (code review F1, `decisions.md` rows of that date): the visible thing wins on both surfaces — a badge's 38 dp disc → a piece's drawn shape (the PG miniature in its cell; a placed piece on the board) → the rest of the badge's 60 dp area (wholly inside the PG cell in the tray) → the rest of a tray cell / a placed piece's 12 dp margin → nothing.*
3. A touch that ends having moved < 12 dp (S-8) is a tap: `pressBadge`, `tapTray` or `tapBoard`. Otherwise, from a cell or a piece, it is a drag: `startDrag` at the drawn centre, then `moveDrag` each frame, with the piece's lowest point 30 dp above the finger (REQ-014) and `overBoard` = the finger is over the board. A touch that starts on a badge never drags.
4. A release calls `drop()`; a pointer cancel calls `goHome()`.

## WO-004 — #Browsing + :store: moving between puzzles, and progress that survives a restart  (Mode: Full (c))

Mode **Full (c)**, a new persistence format (`workorders/WO-004.md` § Mode). The seam table is the reviewed design's: `designs/WO-004-design.md` §2 (S-17…S-24 and the Δ rows over § WO-003's S-1…S-16), with its touch geometry and "What survives". Every seam there names one of the tasks below. Briefs: `workorders/WO-004-briefs.md`. Rows are in landing order.

- **Lanes.**
  - **Test lane:** TASK-016 (the JVM kit) lands first. TASK-019 (the device kit) drafts beside TASK-016…TASK-018 and lands once TASK-017 and TASK-018 are green.
  - **Implementer lane:** TASK-017 ∥ TASK-018 → TASK-020 → TASK-021 → TASK-022. Hardest first: the save path (S-17's atomic replace; S-19/S-20's one ordered writer — design §3 "Highest-risk part"), then the top bar and the touch order, then the overview, then the `:app` start-up and the death channel.
  - The authors' drafting beside other tasks is dispatch, not a plan dependency.
- **`[P]`: TASK-017 ∥ TASK-018.** They sit on opposite sides of the frozen `IProgressStore` (`task-decomposition.md`: governed interfaces make fan-out safe). TASK-017 implements it in `:store`; TASK-018 consumes it in `:play`, tested over the kit's in-memory double (design §4; review F5(a) as triaged). No shared file (`store/` against `play/`; neither touches `gradle/libs.versions.toml` — a needed catalog entry is a recirculation), no shared input (TASK-017's tests read only temporary files). Both are dispatched only after TASK-016 has landed. Their Depends-on cells read "—" because plan-check reads any Depends-on on a `[P]` row as RED; the order is carried by the rows (check 2 reads a Done-check file as landed when an earlier row produces it). Their Gradle runs must not overlap in one working tree (briefs § Dispatch).
- **Task class** (staffing, WO § Constraints; each Description starts with it). Every task goes to the strongest model; none is mechanical. Persistence: TASK-016, TASK-017, TASK-018. Concurrency: TASK-018 (the FIFO writer), TASK-022 (the IO start-up). UI, rendering and touch: TASK-019, TASK-020, TASK-021. Device-only: TASK-019 and TASK-022 (the process-death pair).
- **Four test source sets, each compiled at once by Gradle — the red windows.** Nobody runs a set for a verdict inside its window.
  - `store/src/test`: from TASK-016's landing until TASK-017 lands (unresolved S-17, S-18). `StoreScaffoldTest` cannot run in that window.
  - `play/src/test`: from TASK-016's landing until TASK-018 lands (unresolved S-19, S-1Δ, S-2Δ and the controller side of S-3Δ). The WO-003 JVM kit cannot run in that window.
  - `play/src/androidTest`: the WO-003 device kit still compiles and runs until TASK-019 lands (TASK-018's informational probe uses that). From TASK-019's landing until TASK-020 lands the set does not compile (S-11Δ, S-13Δ, S-14Δ, S-23). From TASK-020 on it compiles; `OverviewKitTest` fails at run time until TASK-021.
  - `app/src/androidTest`: from TASK-019's landing until TASK-022 lands the set does not compile (S-15Δ: `AppGraph.controller` becomes a `StateFlow`), so `FloorChannelTest` cannot run either.
  - Product: `:app` main compiles at every step (seam note 3).
- **Seam notes** (the planner's; no new shape and no changed signature — the design's table governs).
  1. **The death pair's names**, which S-24 leaves open: the phases are the methods `phase1` and `phase2` of `app.tangramplaytime.app.ProcessDeathKitTest`, each guarded by `assumeTrue` on the instrumentation argument `deathPhase` (`1` or `2`). `tools/process_death_check.sh` exits 0 only when phase 1 and then phase 2 pass. Its `--positive-control` runs phase 2 alone after `pm clear` and exits 0 only when that run is judged failed (the V-01 convention: a control passes when the check sees what it must see). A phase skipped by its `assumeTrue` still prints `OK (1 test)`, so the script must never judge a skipped phase as passed.
  2. **S-18's one-instance pin runs on the device**, in the app kit. `onDevice(context)` takes an Android `Context`, which a plain JVM test cannot build (there is no Robolectric or Mockito in the catalog; D5). Design §3 lists the pin with the `:store` JVM test; nothing else about S-18 moves.
  3. **S-15Δ's strings land with S-11Δ in TASK-020**: the ten new `PlayTexts` fields, their `values/` and `values-fi/` resources and their `getString` calls in `MainActivity`, so that `:app` compiles at every step. The rest of S-15Δ is TASK-022's. (Design write-back requested: S-15Δ's "Delivered by" cell.)
  4. **Zones and texts** (S-13Δ: "a control's bounds are its touch area"; S-14Δ: "texts are merged, inert nodes"). `counter` is a zone that does not merge its descendants, so `title`, `rating` and `state-word` keep their tags in the merged tree, and `title` keeps its § WO-003 tag-table row (BoardTrayKitTest reads it). The "n / N" text is its own merged, inert node inside `counter`. The pills (`restart`, `retry`, `best-time`, `solved-next`, `overview-done`) are tagged display containers that merge their label (`platform-notes/android.md` § Compose test kit).
- **Lines covered twice, on purpose** (design §4 "Tokens"; review F5(b) as triaged: no build-map row).
  - REQ-040.A1 and REQ-041.A1 keep their `content/` tokens (WO-002, the code home); the `play/` and `app/` tokens are the screen half. REQ-045.A1 keeps `BoardTrayKitTest` (WO-003); the JVM and app tokens re-cover it over an empty store.
  - REQ-003.A2 and REQ-025.A1 are tokened in `play/` (the #Browsing home: the JVM kit, and the device kit with the recreation leg), and again in `app/` on `ProcessDeathKitTest.phase2` (the process-death leg).
  - The `:store` row-11 test carries no token: its cases are governance row 11 pins of the `IProgressStore` KDoc and the 2026-10-15 ADR-003 rows.
- **In-tree tests this WO changes, and who changes each** (`task-decomposition.md`: declared inputs land together; an implementer never edits a kit file).
  - `PlayControllerKitTest.kt`, the S-2 pin "a Solved puzzle keeps its final placements" → TASK-016 rewrites it to S-21 (design §3: pins on that text go red legitimately).
  - These go to TASK-019, which lands before TASK-020. Each amendment cites its seam row or `decisions.md` row, and no token moves.
    - `KitScreen.kt`'s `PlayTexts` literal (S-11Δ).
    - `DragKitTest.kt` REQ-020.A2 and `CornerPulseKitTest.kt` REQ-051.A2. They snapshot every text and tag on a New puzzle, and the first tray drag now adds `restart` and `state-word`.
    - `SolvedPictureKitTest.kt`: `thePicturesOutlineIsIdenticalToTheSilhouettes` and `noPicturePaintsOutsideItsPuzzlesOutline` capture the board In progress and compare it after the solve, outside the silhouette. The Restart pill is drawn In progress and gone once Solved (S-22 "Restart"; decisions.md 2026-10-15 "The top bar, Restart and the solved row, as drawn").
    - `DecorationKitTest.kt`'s board and tray captures taken In progress. Audit each against the pill and the top bar, and amend it where it crosses them.
    - Any other board pixel sampler that runs In progress (the Restart pill now sits at the board's top-left).
  - `AppLaunchKitTest.kt` (the wait for `play-screen`; the cleared-store precondition) → TASK-019. `FloorChannelTest.kt` (scaffolding: the same wait) → TASK-022.
  - `BoardTrayKitTest.kt`'s title assertion stays valid only while `title` keeps its row (seam note 4) → TASK-020 keeps it.
  - The scaffolding tests under `play/src/test` are the implementers': whoever's change breaks one updates it and says so.
- **Verification channels (G3).** The commands are in the briefs' channel table.
  - JVM: `:store:testDebugUnitTest`, `:play:testDebugUnitTest`, `:content:testDebugUnitTest`, `:contracts:test`.
  - Dev image `Medium_Phone_API_37.0` (running as `emulator-5554`): the play device set (TASK-020, TASK-021) and the app set (TASK-022), one class per invocation or a whole set, each result XML copied to the scratchpad.
  - Floor image `Phone_API_26`: the whole play set (TASK-021), the app set and the death pair (TASK-022). One emulator at a time.
  - Process death: `tools/process_death_check.sh` and its positive control on both images (TASK-022). It is a test-channel harness, not a verifier: it judges acceptance tests across a process boundary that Gradle's connected runner cannot express, it lives in `tools/` beside `tools/device_reset.sh`, and it is not listed in `.swdev/verifiers/manifest.json`. Its line count goes into the WO Metrics as harness lines. WO-004 adds no verifier (budget used: 0 of 1, 0 of 150 lines).
  - The release verifier V-01 with its positive control (TASK-022 and the close).
  - Device hygiene: `bash tools/device_reset.sh` and then `--check` (clean) before **and** after every device step. From this WO on the app writes `no_backup/progress.json` at its first open, so a `--check` straight after a run that leaves the app installed reports NOT clean by design; the after-step resets first. The death script also resets as its last step.
  - Human eyes are not a DoD channel. The owner plays the build at close (`review-notes.md`: the top bar, Restart, the solved row, the grid, the Finnish strings).
- **Not covered here** (no token): REQ-029, REQ-030, REQ-031 (time values, WO-008: `IProgressStore` carries the fields and this WO writes what it is given, O-04); REQ-033, REQ-034 (WO-007: `settings` and `resetAll` carry untokened row-11 pins); REQ-037.A1 (the ‹ › 52 dp are drawn; the sweep is WO-006's); REQ-002.A2 and REQ-039.A2 (WO-009).
- **Unverified by design:** none (design §1 "Pending drafts").

## WO-005 — #DevTools: the passcode-protected solution reveal, in debug builds only  (Mode: Light)

Mode **Light** (`workorders/WO-005.md` § Mode: none of the four reasons holds). There is no design. The planner's seams below are the design, as in § WO-003: the seam table, the aid's tag table, the touch geometry and "What survives". Every seam names its REQ and its delivering task. The five questions of the WO's § Constraints are settled in the seams; each reading that no REQ fixes goes to the orchestrator as a planner question, and the orchestrator writes the `decisions.md` row (titles proposed in the hand-back, cited in the briefs). Briefs: `workorders/WO-005-briefs.md`. Rows are in landing order.

- **Lanes.**
  - **Test lane:** TASK-023 (the JVM kit) lands first. TASK-025 (the device kit) drafts beside TASK-023 and TASK-024 and lands once TASK-024 is green.
  - **Implementer lane:** TASK-024 → TASK-026. Hardest first:
    - TASK-024 holds the novel logic: the debug-only door, the aid's state, and "solve now" driven through the controller's own actions in an order found by the engine.
    - TASK-026 then puts the overlay, the DEV button and the sheet on the screen, places them in the touch order, and wires `:app`.
  - The authors' drafting beside other tasks is dispatch, not a plan dependency.
- **Task class** (staffing; each Description starts with it). Every task goes to the strongest model; none is mechanical.
  - TASK-023: JVM oracles over every bundled puzzle, and a scan of the release APK.
  - TASK-024: novel logic behind a build-variant seam.
  - TASK-025: UI and touch oracles, with device-only legs and pixel colour.
  - TASK-026: UI, rendering and touch.
  - Device-only: TASK-025 and TASK-026.
- **No task is `[P]`.**
  - Every task's tests read the bundled puzzles, a shared declared input.
  - TASK-024 and TASK-026 share the S-25 and S-26 files.
  - Each kit lands between two implementer steps.
- **Red windows.** Gradle compiles each source set at once. Nobody runs a set for a verdict inside its window.
  - `play/src/test`: from TASK-023's landing until TASK-024 lands (unresolved S-25, S-26). The WO-003 and WO-004 JVM kit cannot run in that window.
  - `play/src/androidTest`: from TASK-025's landing until TASK-026 lands (unresolved S-10Δ2). The ten WO-003/004 classes cannot run in that window.
  - `app/src/androidTest`: compiles from TASK-025's landing (S-25's `installed` and S-28 are TASK-024's). `DevAidAppKitTest` fails at run time until TASK-026 wires `MainActivity`.
- **Seam notes** (the planner's, binding like the rows).
  1. **`play/src/test` compiles only for the debug unit-test variant.** AGP 9.4 creates `:play:testDebugUnitTest` and no release unit-test task (`:play:tasks --all`, 2026-10-17). The JVM kit may therefore call the debug source set's `internal` classes. If a later WO adds a release unit-test variant, the aid's JVM tests move to `play/src/testDebug`, and `.swdev/guard.json` `test_globs` gains that folder.
  2. **The build-map cell.** trace-check reads the `#DevTools` code home of `build-map.md` v1.4 literally, as ``play/` (`src/debug`)`` (checked with `parse_build_map`, 2026-10-17). A REQ-046 token under `play/` would therefore fail its code-home rule ("directory theater"). The cell must read `play/` before trace-check runs (planner question 1, a notify-tier delta). The token lists below assume it.
  3. **No WO-003/004 test sees the aid.**
     - `PlayScreen`'s `devAid` defaults to null, and the play device kit composes the screen without one. Only the new tests pass the aid.
     - The app kit runs the real app, which shows the DEV button. Its four classes have no exact tag set, no text snapshot and no board pixel capture; they tap only the counter, and they place pieces through S-2 (grep, 2026-10-17).
     - `ShownControls` gains a defaulted field, so the scaffolding tests that construct it compile unchanged.
  4. **A4 reads build outputs.** `ReleaseBuildKitTest` reads the release and debug APKs of `:app`. The JVM channel line therefore builds and repackages both before `:play:testDebugUnitTest`, and the test refuses a stale APK (the WO-004 lesson in `platform-notes/android.md`).
- **In-tree tests this WO changes, and who changes each.**
  - `KitScreen.kt` gains an optional `devAid`, given to `PlayScreen` in `show()` and `recreate()` → TASK-025.
  - Nothing else of the WO-003/004 kit changes (seam note 3).
  - The scaffolding tests belong to the implementers.
- **Lines covered twice, on purpose.**
  - REQ-046.A1: on the JVM (the aid's state), on the screen, and in the real app (the wiring and `ServiceLoader` on a device).
  - REQ-046.A3: on the JVM (every bundled puzzle, and the save) and on the screen (the picture, the best time).
  - REQ-046.A2: only on the screen. REQ-046.A4: only on the APKs.
- **Unverified by design: none.**
  - The aid solves through the locked TYPE-006 transitions only (S-27), so A3 does not wait on `capture-outbox/chg-draft-type006-missing-transitions.md`. Its end state also matches that draft's sentence.
  - The build meets both the locked REQ-046 and `capture-outbox/chg-draft-req046-android-build-terms.md`: a release build is the `release` build type; the aid stays unlocked until the process restarts; there are no design sheets.
- **Verification channels (G3).** The commands are in the briefs' channel table.
  - JVM: `:play:testDebugUnitTest` (after the line has rebuilt both APKs, seam note 4) and `:contracts:test`. `:store` and `:content` are unchanged and re-run at close.
  - Dev image `Medium_Phone_API_37.0`: the play device set (11 classes) and the app set (5 classes), at TASK-026.
  - Floor image `Phone_API_26`: the same two sets, at TASK-026 and at close. One emulator at a time. `tools/device_reset.sh` and its `--check` run before and after every device step.
  - The release APK: REQ-046.A4's test (the JVM line), and V-01 with its positive control (TASK-024, TASK-026, the close). There is no new verifier: the budget used is 0 of 1, and 0 of 150 lines.
  - Process death: not a channel of this WO. No store path changes, and the aid saves through `Persistence` (S-19). The app set's two guarded skips run as before.
  - Human eyes: not a DoD channel. The owner plays the debug build at close from `review-notes.md` (the DEV button, the prompt, the overlay, "solve now", the Finnish strings). Browser: none.
- **Not covered here** (no token):
  - REQ-023: an aid solve plays it (S-27). It is pinned, and its lines are WO-003's.
  - REQ-022: consumed (the last lock).
  - REQ-037.A1: WO-006's. REQ-037's rule exempts the aid's controls.
  - REQ-024.A2: WO-004's. The bar stays drawn above the sheet, and that is pinned.
  - The TYPE-006 transitions the aid drives are pinned untokened.

### Seams (the planner's, in place of a design seam table)

The door and the screen live in package `app.tangramplaytime.play`, folder `play/src/main/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/play/`. The aid lives in package `app.tangramplaytime.play.devaid`, folder `play/src/debug/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/play/devaid/`; the debug source set is compiled into the `debug` build type only (G-08, O-08). S-1…S-16 are § WO-003's, S-17…S-24 and the Δ rows are `designs/WO-004-design.md` §2's; **Δ2** marks a WO-005 change. The kits call these names: a seam that must change during P4 is a `recirculate → planner`, never a quiet edit.

| # | Seam (file) | Shape | REQ | Called by | Delivered by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-25 | The door (`DevAidHook.kt`, `src/main`) | Public `abstract class DevAidHook internal constructor()`: REQ-046's one door from `src/main` to the debug build's aid. Its KDoc names REQ-046, G-08 and O-08. It holds no aid text, no passcode and no drawing, and it names no aid class.<br>**Members**, all `internal abstract`:<br>- `val sheetOpen: Boolean`: the prompt or the menu is open, so the router stands aside (S-22Δ2);<br>- `fun onButton()`: the DEV button was acted on at its lift;<br>- `@Composable fun rememberButtonSlot(controller: PlayController, geometry: ScreenGeometry): Slot?`: the DEV button's touch area in this frame, or null when it is not shown;<br>- `@Composable fun SolutionLayer(controller: PlayController, geometry: ScreenGeometry)`;<br>- `@Composable fun ButtonLayer(slot: Slot)`;<br>- `@Composable fun SheetLayer(controller: PlayController, area: Slot)`.<br>**Public `companion object { val installed: DevAidHook? }`:** `java.util.ServiceLoader.load(DevAidHook::class.java, DevAidHook::class.java.classLoader).firstOrNull()`, evaluated once per process (`by lazy`). It is the debug build's `DevAid`; in a release build it is null, because there is no service file (S-30).<br>**Harmless when empty:** with null, the screen draws nothing for it, hits nothing for it and never stands aside for it | REQ-046 (G-08, O-08) | S-10Δ2, S-22Δ2, S-15Δ2; JVM kit (`installed`) | TASK-024 (the class, `installed`, `sheetOpen`, `onButton`); TASK-026 (the four composable members) |
| S-26 | The aid (`DevAid.kt` in the aid folder; more files there as needed) | `internal class DevAid : DevAidHook()`, with a public no-arg constructor because `ServiceLoader` builds it. `internal enum class DevSheet { NONE, PROMPT, MENU }`.<br>**Reads:** Compose snapshot state, readable from a JVM test without a composition:<br>- `unlocked: Boolean`;<br>- `solutionShown: Boolean`;<br>- `sheet: DevSheet`;<br>- `wrongPasscode: Boolean` (the prompt shows the "Wrong passcode." line).<br>`sheetOpen` is `sheet != NONE`.<br>**Actions** (main thread):<br>- `onButton()`: PROMPT when locked (with `wrongPasscode` false); MENU when unlocked.<br>- `submit(passcode: String): Boolean`: exactly `"0417"` (REQ-046 statement) gives `unlocked`, MENU, `wrongPasscode` false, and returns true. Anything else, the empty entry included, sets `wrongPasscode`, stays on PROMPT, changes nothing else, and returns false (REQ-046 rule 1).<br>- `close()`: NONE and `wrongPasscode` false (Cancel, Done, a tap on the scrim).<br>- `toggleSolution(): Boolean`: flips `solutionShown` and closes (DI-1). While locked: false and no change.<br>- `solveNow(controller: PlayController): Boolean`: closes, then S-27. While locked: false and no change.<br>A new `DevAid` is locked, with `solutionShown` false and the sheet NONE. The passcode is a `const` in the aid folder and is never shown | REQ-046 A1, rules 1–3 | JVM kit; S-25's composables; device kit (through the screen) | TASK-024 (state, actions); TASK-026 (the composable overrides) |
| S-27 | "Solve this puzzle now" (`DevAid.solveNow`) | REQ-046 A3 and rule 4; TYPE-006 as locked; planner questions 2 and 5. **False, and nothing changes,** when `controller.state` is Solved, or when no order exists. A `controller.boardDpPerUnit` that is not > 0 is a programming error (S-2): `IllegalStateException` before anything changes (G-15).<br>1. **Poses.** For each `SolvedPiece` of `controller.puzzle.solution`:<br>- the centre is the polygon's average (`Placement.centre` KDoc);<br>- the turn and mirror are the first of turns 0..7, unmirrored first and mirrored for the parallelogram only, whose `controller.engine.vertices` equal the polygon as a point set within 1e-6.<br>2. **Order.** Depth-first over the remaining pieces in tray order, from an empty board. At each step, `controller.engine.findLock(puzzle, planned, piece, pose.turn, pose.mirrored, pose.centre, controller.boardDpPerUnit)` must be `Locked` at the pose: the centre within 1e-6, and the same turn and mirror. A set of placed pieces with no way on is not tried again. REQ-019's rationale says such an order exists. The order is planned before anything changes.<br>3. **Drive,** through S-2's public actions only. First `restart()` if In progress (In progress → New). Then, per step:<br>- `pressBadge()` while the parallelogram's tray mirror differs from its pose;<br>- `startDrag(piece, pose.centre)` (the first one moves New → In progress);<br>- `twistDrag`, the shorter way to the pose's turn;<br>- `moveDrag(pose.centre, overBoard = true)`;<br>- `drop()` returns the planned placement. Anything else is a programming error (`IllegalStateException`, G-15).<br>**The last lock is the ordinary solve** (S-2 `drop`): Solved, a new `Celebration`, `Solved`, and the S-20 save through `Persistence`.<br>**Times pass through:** Restart writes 0, as S-2Δ says. The best time is never written: null stays null, and a saved one is kept.<br>**Events:** the ordinary ones (`PickedUp`, `Turned` per step, `Locked`, `Solved`) | REQ-046 A3, rule 4 (TYPE-006; REQ-022, REQ-023 consumed) | S-26; JVM kit; device kit (through the menu) | TASK-024 |
| S-28 | `DevAidTags` (`DevAidTags.kt` in the aid folder) | Public `object DevAidTags`, public because `app/src/androidTest` reads it. It holds the tags of the aid's tag table below, every one declared at TASK-024, plus `fun solution(id: PieceId) = "dev-solution-${id.name}"` and `fun solutionLabel(id: PieceId) = "dev-solution-label-${id.name}"` | REQ-046 | both device kits | TASK-024 |
| S-29 | The aid's strings (`play/src/debug/res/values/strings.xml`, `play/src/debug/res/values-fi/strings.xml`) | Every name starts with `dev_aid_` (planner question 3: O-06 read for the aid).<br>- From REQ-046 (the Contract wording): `dev_aid_button` "DEV" (the statement), `dev_aid_wrong_passcode` "Wrong passcode." (rule 1), `dev_aid_show` "Show the solution" (A2), `dev_aid_solve` "Solve this puzzle now" (A3).<br>- After DI-1, adapted (wording in the hand-back for `review-notes.md`): `dev_aid_title`, `dev_aid_passcode_hint`, `dev_aid_cancel`, `dev_aid_ok`, `dev_aid_unlocked_hint`, `dev_aid_hide`, `dev_aid_no_best_time`, `dev_aid_done`.<br>Finnish is a reference translation (G-10). The overlay's labels are `PieceId.name`, which is data, as a puzzle title is, not a resource. No aid string goes into `:app` | REQ-046 (G-10) | S-26's composables; device kits (the English) | TASK-024 |
| S-30 | Discovery and markers (`play/src/debug/resources/META-INF/services/app.tangramplaytime.play.DevAidHook`) | The service file holds one line, `app.tangramplaytime.play.devaid.DevAid`.<br>**The markers REQ-046.A4's test reads:**<br>(a) class descriptors under `Lapp/tangramplaytime/play/devaid/` in `classes*.dex`;<br>(b) resource names starting `dev_aid_` in `resources.arsc`;<br>(c) the zip entry `META-INF/services/app.tangramplaytime.play.DevAidHook`.<br>Every aid class lives in that package, and every aid string carries that prefix. `src/main` holds none of (a)–(c). `play/src/debug/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/play/DebugCanary.kt` stays where V-01 reads it | REQ-046 A4 (G-08) | `ReleaseBuildKitTest`; S-25 `installed` | TASK-024 |
| S-10Δ2 | `PlayScreen` (`PlayScreen.kt`) | `PlayScreen(controller, layout, texts, reducedMotion, modifier = Modifier, devAid: DevAidHook? = null)`. With an aid:<br>- `rememberButtonSlot` runs at each composition, and its slot is published in `ShownControls.devButton` with the other controls (the router hits exactly what is drawn);<br>- `SolutionLayer` comes right after the silhouette;<br>- `ButtonLayer`, when the slot is not null, comes after the solved row and before the landing preview;<br>- `SheetLayer` comes last, over `Slot(0, bar bottom, W, H − bar bottom)`, while `sheetOpen` holds.<br>Without an aid, the screen is exactly WO-004's | REQ-046 | device kit; S-15Δ2 | TASK-026 |
| S-22Δ2 | Touch and z-order (`HitTest.kt`, `PointerRouter.kt`) | `Control.DEV_BUTTON`; `ShownControls(restart, solvedRow, devButton: Slot? = null)`. `PointerRouter` gains a `devAid: DevAidHook?` constructor argument:<br>- hit order 0 also holds while `devAid?.sheetOpen`;<br>- `act(DEV_BUTTON)` is `devAid.onButton()`.<br>The geometry, the z-order and the hit order are given below | REQ-046 rules 1, 5; S-22 | device kit; scaffolding tests | TASK-026 |
| S-15Δ2 | `:app` (`MainActivity.kt`) | `PlayScreen(…, devAid = DevAidHook.installed)`. Nothing else in `:app` changes: no aid string, no `app/src/debug`, no `app/src/release` | REQ-046 | app kit | TASK-026 |

**The aid's tag table** (S-28 names). A node's presence follows the aid's state and the controller at once.

| Tag (`DevAidTags`) | Node | Present while | Bounds | Keys or text |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dev-button` (`DEV_BUTTON`) | the DEV pill; a merged, inert display container | the open puzzle is not Solved | the drawn pill, which is its touch area | its text, "DEV" |
| `dev-solution-<ID>` (`solution(id)`) | one overlay shape per piece of the open puzzle | `solutionShown` holds and the puzzle is not Solved | the px box of the piece's solution polygon | `PieceKey` |
| `dev-solution-label-<ID>` (`solutionLabel(id)`) | the piece's id; merged, inert text | with its shape | the text | its text, `PieceId.name` |
| `dev-aid-sheet` (`SHEET`) | the scrim and the sheet | `sheet` is not NONE | the screen below the top bar | — |
| `dev-aid-prompt` (`PROMPT`) | the prompt's content | PROMPT | the content | — |
| `dev-aid-passcode` (`PASSCODE`) | the masked numeric field | PROMPT | the field | its editable text |
| `dev-aid-message` (`MESSAGE`) | "Wrong passcode."; merged, inert | PROMPT and `wrongPasscode` | the text | its text |
| `dev-aid-ok`, `dev-aid-cancel` (`OK`, `CANCEL`) | the prompt's pills: merged containers with a click action | PROMPT | the pill, which is its touch area | the label |
| `dev-aid-menu` (`MENU`) | the menu's content | MENU | the content | — |
| `dev-aid-show` (`SHOW`) | "Show the solution", or "Hide the solution" while `solutionShown` | MENU | the pill | the label |
| `dev-aid-solve` (`SOLVE`) | "Solve this puzzle now" | MENU | the pill | the label |
| `dev-aid-done` (`DONE`) | "Done" | MENU | the pill | the label |

**Touch geometry (S-22Δ2, phone).** The numbers are DI-1's (`.devbtn`, `.devbtn.on`, `#devDlg`, `.pin`, `drawHint`). TASK-026 records any number that no line here fixes as a proposed `decisions.md` row.
- **The DEV button.**
  - **Place:** the board's bottom-left. Its left edge is 10 dp right of the board's left edge, and its bottom edge 10 dp above the board's bottom edge (DI-1 `left: b.x + 10`, `top: b.y + b.h − 38`). This is the REQ-046 ASSUMPTION and checkpoint item 1.
  - **Size:** 28 dp high, as wide as its label plus 10 dp of padding and the 1.5 dp outline on each side, fully rounded. The label is "DEV", 11 sp bold, letter spacing 0.8 sp.
  - **Look, off:** a dashed 1.5 dp `PlayPalette.controlEdge` outline, no fill, the label in `PlayPalette.muted`, all at 75 % opacity.
  - **Look, while `solutionShown`:** a solid `PlayPalette.accent` outline and label, opaque.
  - **Touch area:** the pill itself. REQ-037's rule exempts the #DevTools controls.
  - It is neither drawn nor hit while the open puzzle is Solved (REQ-046 rule 5).
- **The overlay.**
  - For each `SolvedPiece` of the open puzzle, its polygon in px through `BoardMapping` (G-17).
  - The fill is `PlayPalette.piece(id)` at 55 % alpha. The edge is white, 2 dp, dashed 6 dp on and 4 dp off, with round joins.
  - The label is `id.name`, white, 14 sp bold, centred on the polygon's average.
  - It is not touchable.
  - It is not drawn while the open puzzle is Solved. `solutionShown` is kept, so the overlay returns on the next unsolved puzzle.
- **The sheet.** As the overview is built:
  - The scrim (`PlayPalette.scrim`) and the sheet start below the top bar. The bar stays drawn and inert.
  - The sheet is centred, inset 16 dp, at most 360 dp wide, in the paper colour, with 26 dp corners. Its pills are at least 48 dp high.
  - **Prompt:** the title, the hint, the field, the message line, Cancel and OK. The field shows masked digits, uses the system's number keyboard, and takes focus when the prompt opens. A wrong passcode empties it.
  - **Menu:** the title, the unlocked hint, Show (or Hide) the solution, Solve this puzzle now, the no-best-time hint, and Done.
  - A tap on the scrim, Cancel or Done closes it (`close()`). Show, Hide and Solve close it before they act (S-26). Back is not handled (planner question 6).
- **Z-order,** bottom to top: board → silhouette → **the overlay** → Restart pill → board pieces → solved picture → board badge → tray strip, cells, ghosts, miniatures and size marks → tray badge → solved row → **the DEV button** → landing preview → dragged piece → corner pulse → confetti → overview → **the sheet**.
- **Hit order** (S-22's, with the aid):
  0. The overview **or the aid's sheet** is open: the router neither consumes nor acts, on the top bar too. The sheet's `clickable`s and its field act.
  1. A drag is running: every further touch is a twist finger, a finger on the DEV button included.
  2. A top-bar column, or a third of the solved row.
  3. **The DEV button.** It is drawn above every resting piece and badge, and the visible thing wins. Then the badge disc → a piece's drawn shape → the Restart pill → the rest of the badge area → a cell or a margin.
- **Taps.** The DEV button is a control: it never drags. It acts at the lift within the 12 dp slop, whatever the duration. A slide of the slop or more cancels it, and it has no long press (S-22 "Taps and the long press").

**What survives.**
- **An activity recreation** keeps the aid object. `DevAidHook.installed` is built once per process, so `unlocked`, `solutionShown`, the open sheet and its message survive; what was typed in the field may be lost. The controller keeps the rest (WO-004). A celebration that an aid solve started is a controller cue and is not replayed (S-3Δ).
- **A process death** builds a new aid: locked, the overlay off, no sheet. This is "until the app is restarted", REQ-046 rule 2 as read (the pending draft's wording). An aid-solved puzzle is in the store as Solved with its best time unchanged. It therefore opens Solved: the picture, the solved row, and the best time "–".
- **Leaving the app** without a process death changes nothing in the aid.

## WO-006 — #Layout + #Language + #Promise: phone and tablet layouts, insets and Back, touch sizes, the en/fi texts, the free-and-offline promise  (Mode: Light)

Mode **Light** (`workorders/WO-006.md` § Mode: none of the four reasons holds — no new module, every governed interface consumed, no persistence change, no hard-stop domain). There is no design: § Seams below is the design, as in § WO-003 and § WO-005 — the seam table, the touch geometry per layout class, the player controls per screen and "What survives a rotation". The WO's eight questions are settled there; each reading no REQ fixes goes to the orchestrator as a planner question (Q1–Q9, titles proposed in the hand-back, cited in the briefs), and the orchestrator writes the `decisions.md` row. Briefs: `workorders/WO-006-briefs.md`. Rows are in landing order.

- **Lanes.**
  - **Test lane:** TASK-027 (the JVM kit) lands first. TASK-030 (the device kit, all in the app) drafts beside TASK-027…TASK-029 and lands once TASK-029 is green (the same author, context resumed).
  - **Hygiene:** TASK-028 (the reset script) runs beside TASK-027.
  - **Implementer lane:** TASK-029 → TASK-031 → TASK-032. Hardest first: the safe-area geometry under every screen, which must leave an inset-free screen exactly as WO-005 left it; then the window shell, whose behaviour depends on the API level and the device class (orientation, edge to edge, the keyboard); then the language override.
  - The author's drafting beside other tasks is dispatch, not a plan dependency.
- **Task class** (staffing; each Description starts with it). Every task goes to the strongest model; none is mechanical. UI and rendering: TASK-029. Device-only: TASK-028, TASK-030, TASK-031, TASK-032.
- **`[P]`: TASK-027 ∥ TASK-028.** No dependency, no shared file (the kit's three new test files against `tools/device_reset.sh`), no shared declared input (TASK-028's script reads device state only). Every other pair is serial: TASK-029, TASK-031 and TASK-032 share the screen and `MainActivity.kt`, and every test task after TASK-029 reads the bundled puzzles.
- **Red windows.** Gradle compiles each source set at once. Nobody runs a set for a verdict inside its window.
  - `kernel/src/test`: from TASK-027's landing until TASK-029 lands (unresolved S-34). The WO-001 kernel kit cannot run in that window.
  - `play/src/test`: from TASK-027's landing until TASK-029 lands (unresolved S-31, S-32's new fields). The WO-003…005 JVM kit cannot run in that window.
  - `app/src/test`: new in this WO; it compiles at TASK-027's landing (its one file reads only build outputs).
  - `app/src/androidTest`: compiles at TASK-030's landing — no new class calls a new `:app` seam (seam note 2). Its five new test classes fail at run time until TASK-031 (layout, touch, play-through) and TASK-032 (the `[sv, fi]` language leg).
  - `play/src/androidTest`: untouched by the kit; it compiles throughout (S-33's signature changes stay inside `:play` main and debug code).
- **Seam notes** (the planner's, binding like the rows).
  1. **No insets means WO-005's screen.** `PlayLayout`'s new fields are defaulted (S-31), so every existing literal compiles and means what it meant. With `PlayInsets.NONE` and no keyboard every slot of `ScreenGeometry` equals WO-005's (S-32), so the eleven play device classes and the scaffolding pins keep their meaning unchanged.
  2. **The device kit is a black box.** It reads nodes (`PlayTags`, `PlaySemantics`, `DevAidTags`), the controller through `AppGraph.controller` (the kits' one door, S-15Δ), the window insets from the launched activity, and it changes device state only through `UiAutomation` (`executeShellCommand`, `setRotation`). It calls no new `:app` seam, so it compiles against the tree as TASK-029 leaves it. Nothing it does needs a permission in the app's manifest (G-01).
  3. **Every app-kit class leaves the controller and the device as it found them** (the WO-004 rule, extended). The open puzzle and every state it changed come back; the overview and the DEV sheet are closed; the display size and density, the rotation, airplane mode, the device and app locales and `show_ime_with_hard_keyboard` are restored in `finally`. `AppLaunchKitTest` still claims the fresh start and `DevAidAppKitTest.kitAssertEnglish` still fails loudly on a non-English device. `tools/device_reset.sh` (S-37) is the safety net, and its `--check` names any leftover.
  4. **A size the device does not have is constructed, never skipped** (`platform-notes/android.md`, the WO-004 lesson). Exact sizes come from a display override (`wm size` + `wm density`) set by the kit and read back from the launched activity; on a device that already has the size (the tablet image at 1280 × 800 dp), nothing is overridden. Portrait on a tablet is a frozen rotation (`UiAutomation.setRotation`).
  5. **Locale and airplane legs per API level** (`decisions.md` 2026-10-17, the Q7 and Q8 rows as amended by "The floor routes, as measured — amends the Q7 and Q8 rows"). On the two API 37 images the kit sets both itself: `cmd locale` (the device locale `fi-FI` or `sv-SE`, the app's list `[sv-SE, fi-FI]`) and `cmd connectivity airplane-mode`. On the floor `Phone_API_26`, `UiAutomation`'s shell has neither route, so **the floor channel line sets them through TASK-028's measured root routes**, each run before its Gradle invocation and read back, with a reset and a clean `--check` around each step:
     - **Locales:** `-change-locale` boots do not take on API 26. The root locale route (the measured row's steps, ending `stop; start`, read back with `am get-config`, then `adb unroot`) runs before Gradle, because the framework restart kills running apps. It sets a list, so the floor runs `LanguageKitTest`'s own-locale leg alone three times, with the instrumentation argument `kitDeviceLocales` = `fi-FI`, `sv-SE` and `sv-SE,fi-FI`. The leg asserts the device's list starts with the named locales before it asserts the rule, so a route that did not take fails loudly. The same route with `en-US` restores the floor.
     - **The `[sv, fi]` list leg** of the `cmd locale` legs carries `@SdkSuppress(minSdkVersion = 33)` and is filtered on the floor; its case is covered there by the third own-locale run. It is not a skip.
     - **Airplane mode:** the root route (`adb root`, then the setting and the `AIRPLANE_MODE` broadcast as root, the radios read back off) runs from the channel line's `adb shell`, never from the kit: `UiAutomation`'s shell is the shell user, and the broadcast is protected. The channel line switches it on, runs `PlayThroughKitTest` alone with `kitAirplaneByChannel=true` — the kit then requires airplane mode on, read back, for the whole run — switches it off the same way and runs `adb unroot`.
     - **The named guarded skips** are `workorders/WO-006.md` § Checkpoint items, last paragraph: only the two `ProcessDeathKitTest` phases. Any other skip in an XML is a failure.
  6. **The run log's trees grow** (`.swdev/run-log/WO-006.md` header): the test tree adds `kernel/src/test` and `app/src/test`; the product tree adds `kernel/src/main` and `kernel/build.gradle.kts`. The JVM line's run 2 adds `:kernel:test` and `:app:testDebugUnitTest`.
- **In-tree tests this WO changes:** none. Both kits only add files. The scaffolding tests under `play/src/test` (`ControlsScaffoldTest`, `DecorationScaffoldTest`, `DevButtonHitScaffoldTest` build `ScreenGeometry` without insets) belong to the implementers: whoever's change breaks one updates it and says so.
- **Lines covered twice, on purpose:** REQ-010.A2 — the release APK's manifest (JVM, TASK-027) and the installed app's package info on every image (device, TASK-030).
- **Unverified by design:** REQ-047.A1 (TASK-032), on `capture-outbox/chg-draft-req047-english-contract.md` (`decisions.md` 2026-10-17 "REQ-047.A1 carries `implemented (unverified)` …"; the planner does not contest it: G-10 forbids asserting a Finnish word, and the kit compares each text with its `fi` resource and the title with the file's `title.fi`). REQ-006.A1 is verified as locked under planner Q9 unless the orchestrator rules otherwise.
- **Verification channels (G3).** The commands are in the briefs' channel table.
  - JVM: the two-run line — run 1 builds and repackages both APKs; run 2 `:play:testDebugUnitTest :contracts:test :kernel:test :app:testDebugUnitTest`, each `--rerun`. `:store` and `:content` are unchanged and re-run at close.
  - Dev image `Medium_Phone_API_37.0` with gesture navigation on (`settings get secure navigation_mode` = 2, recorded with each run): the app set (eleven classes after TASK-030) and the play set (eleven classes).
  - Floor image `Phone_API_26`: the app set and the play set; then, through the root routes, `LanguageKitTest`'s own-locale leg under `fi-FI`, `sv-SE` and `sv-SE,fi-FI`, the `en-US` restore, and `PlayThroughKitTest` with airplane mode on (seam note 5).
  - Tablet image `Medium_Tablet_API_37.0` (1280 × 800 dp, landscape): the app set. The play set is not run there: it hosts `PlayScreen` with the kit's phone literal, and nothing the tablet changes is under test in it.
  - The release APK: `ReleasePromiseKitTest` (TASK-027) and `ReleaseBuildKitTest` (WO-005) in the JVM line; V-01 with its positive control. No new verifier (budget 0 of 1, 0 of 150 lines).
  - Device hygiene: `bash tools/device_reset.sh` and its `--check` before and after every device step, with S-37's device-level items from TASK-028 on.
  - Process death: not a channel of this WO (no store path changes); the app set's two guarded `ProcessDeathKitTest` skips run as before.
  - Human eyes: not a DoD channel. The owner plays the build on a phone and on a tablet at close (`review-notes.md`: the edge-to-edge look, the tablet row, turning the tablet mid-puzzle, the Finnish strings with the device in Finnish). Browser: none.
- **Not covered here** (no token): REQ-009 (the free note) → WO-007; REQ-001.A2 (store labels) → WO-009 (`decisions.md` 2026-10-17 "Scope of WO-006 …"). The settings screen joins the every-screen tests in WO-007 (a carry line at this close). REQ-013.A1 stays WO-003's (`TrayMetricsKitTest`); REQ-050's 64 dp grid cells and REQ-018's 60 dp badge are pinned in the sweep without a token of theirs.

### Seams (the planner's, in place of a design seam table)

Package `app.tangramplaytime.play` (folder `play/src/main/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/play/`), `app.tangramplaytime.app` (folder `app/src/main/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/app/`) and `app.tangramplaytime.kernel` (folder `kernel/src/main/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/kernel/`), as each row says. S-1…S-16 are § WO-003's, S-17…S-24 and the Δ rows `designs/WO-004-design.md` §2's, S-25…S-30 and the Δ2 rows § WO-005's. The kits call these names: a seam that must change during P4 is a `recirculate → planner`, never a quiet edit.

| # | Seam (file) | Shape | REQ | Called by | Delivered by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-31 | `PlayInsets`, `PlayLayout`Δ (`PlayLayout.kt`) | Public `data class PlayInsets(val topDp: Float, val bottomDp: Float, val leftDp: Float, val rightDp: Float)` with `companion object { val NONE = PlayInsets(0f, 0f, 0f, 0f) }`. `PlayLayout` gains, after `topBarDp`, `val insets: PlayInsets = PlayInsets.NONE` and `val keyboardDp: Float = 0f`.<br>- `insets`: the system bars and the display cutout, dp per side — never the keyboard.<br>- `keyboardDp`: the soft keyboard's bottom inset in dp, 0 while it is closed.<br>`:play` still never receives the window size (O-05): insets are part of the layout `:app` decides | REQ-035 rule 3, REQ-036 rule 2; O-05 | S-32, S-33, S-35; JVM kit | TASK-029 |

**Touch geometry per layout class** (S-22's, moved inside the insets by S-32; the class is S-34's).

| Element | Phone (upright width < 600 dp; portrait only) | Tablet (≥ 600 dp; both orientations) | Touch area | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Top bar | 66 dp, below the safe top | 74 dp, below the safe top | — | S-5, S-35, S-32 |
| ‹ and › | full-height columns, 60 dp wide, from the safe left / to the safe right edge; a 52 dp square drawn 8 dp in | the same columns, 74 dp tall | the column, ≥ 52 dp both ways (REQ-037 rule 1) | S-22, S-32; `decisions.md` 2026-10-15 "The top bar's drawing numbers" |
| Counter (opens the grid) | between the columns, the bar's full height | the same | the zone, ≥ 48 dp | S-22 |
| Restart | a 48 dp pill at the board's top-left + 10 dp, In progress only | the same | the pill's slot, ≥ 48 dp | S-22 |
| Tray cells | two rows, LT1 LT2 MT / SQ PG ST1 ST2 (REQ-035 rule 1); gap 8, side margin 8, inside the safe area | one row of all seven in TYPE-001 order (REQ-036 statement); gap 14, side margin 16 | the whole cell (REQ-015 rule, REQ-037 rule 2), ≥ 48 dp | S-6, S-32, S-35 |
| Flip badge | the PG cell's top-right 60 × 60 dp; beside the placed PG on the board | the same | ≥ 60 dp (REQ-037 rule 1, REQ-018 rule), less what a drawn piece covers | `decisions.md` 2026-10-12 "Inside the parallelogram's tray cell the visible thing wins, as on the board" and "What 'drawn' means in the touch order" |
| Solved row | Retry · best time (inert) · Next: thirds of the band between the tray's top and the safe bottom, across the safe width | the same | Retry and Next ≥ 48 dp | S-22, S-32 |
| Grid overview | the scrim below the bar to the edges; the sheet inset 16 dp inside `overlayContent`; cells ≥ 84 dp; Done ≥ 52 dp high | the same | cells ≥ 48 dp (and ≥ 64 dp, REQ-050 rule); Done ≥ 48 dp; the scrim | S-22, S-33; `decisions.md` 2026-10-17 "The grid overview, drawn in detail" |
| DEV button and sheet | as WO-005; the sheet inside `sheetContent` | the same | exempt (REQ-037 rule 3) | § WO-005; S-33 |
| Board pieces | REQ-015's shape grown by 12 dp | the same | not a player control in REQ-037's sense: REQ-015 fixes their area (planner Q5) | REQ-015 |

*Informative arithmetic of S-6, never an oracle (the kit reads nodes):* phone 360 × 780 dp → 24.2 dp/unit, the smallest cell (SQ) 60.3 dp; 390 × 844 → 26.8 / 65.6; 411 × 914 → 28.6 / 69.3; at the pending draft's 320 dp width → 20.7 / 53.3, so no control falls below 48 dp there (REQ-013.A1's 56 dp names 360 dp only). Tablet 1280 × 800 and 800 × 1280 → 27.5 dp/unit, the smallest cell 67.0 dp, the tray 156 dp tall.

**The player controls per screen** (REQ-037.A1's sweep, on each layout class: the phone at 360 × 780 dp, the tablet at 1280 × 800 and 800 × 1280 dp).
- **Play screen, New:** `nav-prev`, `nav-next`, `counter`; every `tray-cell-<ID>`; `flip-badge-tray` when the puzzle has the parallelogram.
- **Play screen, In progress:** the same, plus `restart`, and `flip-badge-board` while the parallelogram is placed.
- **Solved view:** `nav-prev`, `nav-next`, `counter`, `retry`, `solved-next`. `best-time` is inert, not a control.
- **Grid overview:** every `overview-cell-<id>`, `overview-done`, and the scrim (the `overview` node outside the sheet). The top bar is inert while it is open (S-22 hit order 0), so it is not a control then.
- **Exempt:** every `dev-*` node (REQ-037 rule 3).
- **Size:** each listed node's bounds, which S-13Δ and the tag tables declare to be its touch area, in dp with the density read from the launched activity.
- **Probe:** a tap at each listed node's four corners, 1 dp inside, makes that control act — the router's own areas for ‹, ›, the counter, Restart, Retry, Next, the cells and the badges; the foundation `clickable`s for the grid's cells, Done and the scrim. A corner that a drawn shape owns by the visible-thing-wins rows (the PG cell's top-right badge area, a corner on a drawn piece) is not a probe point of that control. Positions are read on the screen in use and asserted inside the region meant (the WO-005 lesson).
- **Completeness:** every node with a click action in the tree is in this list or is a `dev-*` node.

**What survives a rotation** (and every other activity recreation: a device language change, a display override, a window resize).
- **Kept** — everything the process-wide controller holds (`designs/WO-004-design.md` §2 "What survives"):
  - the open puzzle and its TYPE-006 state;
  - every placed piece at its placement in puzzle units (REQ-036 rule 1, A2), redrawn through the new `BoardMapping`;
  - the tray turns and the parallelogram's tray mirror (held in the controller; never saved, `decisions.md` 2026-10-11 F36);
  - the overview's open state;
  - the DEV aid's state — unlocked, the overlay on or off, an open sheet; typed digits may be lost (§ WO-005 "What survives");
  - the cue serials: a pulse, shake or celebration set before the new composition counts as shown, so a just-solved puzzle stands at the end of its timeline with its solved row (S-3Δ).
- **Ended:** a running drag — `onStop` sends it home to its cell, without a pulse, and the board is saved (`decisions.md` 2026-10-12, the TASK-015 row; `capture-outbox/chg-draft-req020-interrupted-drag.md`); a › hold (cancelled); any touch in flight (cancelled, no action).
- **Recomputed:** the layout class (unchanged by a rotation: it reads the upright width, S-34), the orientation request, the insets and the keyboard inset, `ScreenGeometry` and `boardDpPerUnit`.
- **The store:** a rotation writes nothing beyond what a drag's going home saves.
- **A phone does not turn:** under a landscape rotation request its window stays taller than wide (REQ-035 statement), and nothing above changes.
