## WO-010 — Content batch: the TangramCollections figures as new `Tangrams/*.json` puzzles  (Mode: Light)

Mode **Light** (`workorders/WO-010.md` § Mode: none of the four reasons holds — the converter is a content tool in `tools/` (DI-5), not a module; `IPuzzleLibrary` and the `tangram-puzzle/1` format are consumed as locked; no persistence change; no hard-stop domain). There is no design: § Seams below is the design, as in § WO-003, § WO-005 and § WO-006. It settles the WO's ten questions. Each reading no REQ fixes goes to the orchestrator as a planner reading R1–R10 (titles proposed in the hand-back, cited by those titles in the briefs); the orchestrator writes the `decisions.md` row. Two product-visible readings are proposed as checkpoint items C1 and C2; the build follows their defaults. Briefs: `workorders/WO-010-briefs.md`. Rows are in landing order.

- **Lanes.**
  - **Test lane:** TASK-033 (kit stage 1: the corrected 13-bundle pin and the counter pin) lands first. TASK-041 (kit stage 2: the asset record and the colour rule; the same author, context resumed) lands after the four picture batches.
  - **Implementer lane:** TASK-034 (the counter's digits) → TASK-035 (the scale instrument and the 13-puzzle baseline) → TASK-036 (the converter, every accepted figure with a stub picture, the shared files, an early floor run) → TASK-037 ∥ TASK-038 ∥ TASK-039 ∥ TASK-040 (the pictures, by category) → TASK-042 (the scale record and every channel on the final tree).
  - **Hardest first.** TASK-036 carries the WO's risk: the snap, both validity checks over about 93 new geometries, the duplicates, every field, and a floor run of the geometry- and touch-sensitive device classes plus the measurements on the real geometry, all before any picture is drawn. TASK-034 and TASK-035 go before it only because the counter pin should not stay red across the WO, and because the baseline must be measured on the 13-puzzle bundle.
  - The author's drafting of stage 2 beside other tasks is dispatch, not a plan dependency.
- **Task class** (staffing; each Description starts with it). Every station runs on Opus. JVM kit: TASK-033, TASK-041. Mechanical product fix: TASK-034. Mechanical data and tool work with exact geometry: TASK-036 (with a floor-image step). Visual (pictures): TASK-037…TASK-040. Device-only: TASK-035, TASK-042.
- **`[P]`: TASK-037 ∥ TASK-038 ∥ TASK-039 ∥ TASK-040.** No dependency between them. No shared file: each changes only the `art` object of the files in its own list (S-40) and writes only those files' sheets. No shared declared input: each checks only its own files with the Python tools, and none runs Gradle. Their Depends-on cells read "—" because plan-check reads any Depends-on on a `[P]` row as RED (the § WO-004 precedent). They are dispatched only after TASK-036 has landed; the rows carry the order. Every other pair is serial: each reads the bundle `Tangrams/*.json` through `:content`, or changes it.
- **Windows.** Nobody runs a set for a verdict inside its window.
  - **Flux window:** from TASK-036's landing until the last batch lands, the bundle's pictures are half drawn. No JVM line and no device set runs for a verdict then; TASK-041 and TASK-042 come after it.
  - **Run-time red:** `CounterDigitsKitTest` fails from TASK-033's landing until TASK-034 lands. `play/src/test` compiles throughout (S-11Δ2 changes no signature).
  - No other window: the stage-2 pins land after the files they read.
- **Seam notes** (the planner's, binding like the rows).
  1. **Puzzles are added, never changed.** No shipped puzzle file, id, rating or sheet changes; saved progress is keyed on ids. TASK-036 never overwrites a file, and a batch changes nothing but `art`.
  2. **Every figure is accounted for.** 144 = imported + skipped, each skip with its reason (S-38), listed in `Tangrams/README.md` with the counts by reason (the G4 surface). *Planner probe, 2026-10-18 (informative, never an oracle):* about 93 imported. Skipped: 26 not edge-connected (V5: a corner-only join), 14 with a piece off TYPE-003's turns (15° and 30° multiples), 2 with no clean snap, 9 duplicates. Six turned copies are kept and flagged (C1). Both validity rules that turn figures away are locked (REQ-038, TYPE-003): no figure is reshaped to pass.
  3. **The run log's trees** (`.swdev/run-log/WO-010.md` header) grow: the product tree adds `ASSET-LICENCES.md` (TASK-041's pin reads it); the tools tree adds `tools/tests/` and `tools/import/`.
- **In-tree tests this WO changes** (Q7): the REQ-040.A2 test in `content/src/test/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/content/PuzzleLoaderKitTest.kt` (its 13-bundle line; TASK-033, a corrected check), and the folder-equality line of the tool test `tools/tests/test_tangram_geom.py` (TASK-036). Every other test that reads the bundle iterates it and keeps its meaning. Scaffolding tests that pick "the first full puzzle" belong to the implementers: whoever's change breaks one updates it and says so.
- **Unverified by design:** REQ-038.A1 (TASK-036, TASK-042) on `capture-outbox/chg-draft-req038-validity-rules-in-contract.md`. REQ-038.A2 stays outside the `--wo` scope, with the validator's build orders as tool-side evidence (`decisions.md` 2026-10-11, the WO-002 row on REQ-038.A2). REQ-002.A1 (a regression line) stays on its draft.
- **Verification channels (G3).** The commands are in the briefs' channel table.
  - **Python tool channel:** the validator over `Tangrams`; the renderer over the whole folder into the scratch, and per imported file into `Tangrams/previews/` (R4); the two tool tests; S-42's colour report.
  - **Eyes (the AI's, owner rule 7 "look at the sheet"):** a PNG copy of each sheet made by headless Chrome in the scratch, then opened. `tools/svg2png.py` needs Playwright, which is not installed; the Chrome route was probed by the planner on a committed sheet (it works).
  - **JVM:** the two-run line of the run log (g3-jvm).
  - **Device:** the floor image `Phone_API_26` first, then the dev image `Medium_Phone_API_37.0`, then the tablet image `Medium_Tablet_API_37.0`, one emulator at a time. The play set runs on the floor and dev images, the app set on all three. TASK-036 runs two classes on the floor; TASK-042 runs the whole sets once, on the final tree. WO-006's floor root-route steps (a)–(e) are not WO-010 channels (R10).
  - **Release:** V-01 and its positive control.
  - **Not DoD channels:** human eyes (the owner's play-test and review: REQ-039 rule 2, WO-009); a browser beyond the PNG renderer (`tools/tests/test_prototype.py` is not run, R4).
- **Not covered here** (no token): REQ-039.A2 and REQ-042.A1 → WO-009 (the owner's review marks). REQ-038.A2: see above. The regression lines that iterate the bundle (REQ-002.A1, REQ-003.A1, REQ-011.A1, REQ-023.A2, REQ-026.A1, REQ-037.A1, REQ-046.A3, REQ-050) run on their own WOs' tokens, as a channel. The counter's digits get an untokened pin: REQ-047.A2's letter (Swedish) does not reach digits, and its token stays WO-006's (R9).

### Seams (the planner's, in place of a design seam table)

S-1…S-37 are § WO-003's to § WO-006's (with `designs/WO-004-design.md` §2). The rows below are WO-010's. A seam that must change during P4 is a `recirculate → planner`, never a quiet edit.

| # | Seam (file) | Shape | REQ | Called by | Delivered by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-38 | The converter (`tools/import_figures.py`, new) | `python tools/import_figures.py import --convex <convex-13 figures.json> --android <android-tangram-131 figures.json> --report <scratch file> [--dry-run]`. Python 3, stdlib only; it imports `tools/tangram_geom.py` and `tools/validate_puzzles.py`. It stays in the repo as the record of how each file was made, and never writes under `C:/GitHub/AI/TangramCollections`. It takes every figure, convex-13 in file order, then android-tangram-131 in file order:<br>1. **Pieces:** `L` → LT1, the second `L` → LT2; `M` → MT; `S` → ST1, the second `S` → ST2; `Q` → SQ; `P` → PG. The collection's `mirrored` flag is not read: the polygon decides the mirror (TYPE-003; owner rule 12: a figure that needs the mirrored parallelogram is an ordinary puzzle).<br>2. **Snap:** each coordinate v, in the collection's units (small-triangle legs 1, y down, the bounding box from (0, 0)), becomes the one pair (p, q), p and q multiples of ¼ between −16 and 16, whose value p + q√2 lies within 2·10⁻⁴ of v (the files store four decimals). No such pair, or more than one → skipped, reason **no clean snap** (naming the value).<br>3. **Scale:** the exact point times √2 is the `Spec/03-puzzle-format.md` §1 point (TYPE-001: the set assembles into the 4 × 4 square of area 16; the collection's set has area 8). √2·(p + q√2) = 2q + p√2 is written `[2q, p]`, or a plain number when p = 0. No turn, no mirror, no shift: both use y down.<br>4. **Pose:** `tangram_geom.placement_from_polygon` finds each piece's turn, mirror and anchor exactly. None → skipped, reason **a piece off TYPE-003's turns** (V3). The written polygon is `piece_polygon(piece, rot, flip, at)`, the local shape's corner order, so the Kotlin check sees boundary order (`decisions.md` 2026-10-11 "What 'congruent to its piece' means"). Entries follow TYPE-001 order.<br>5. **Screen:** `validate_puzzles.validate` runs on the draft (S-39's fields and the stub picture). An error → skipped, with its rule as the reason (**V5 not edge-connected**: REQ-038 rule 2, a corner-only join; V4, V6 …).<br>6. **Duplicates (Q2):** the silhouette is the union of the solution pieces, never the solution (`Spec/03` §2: any exact cover wins). It is compared exactly, in ℚ(√2), with the 13 shipped puzzles and with every figure accepted before it. Equal after a translation → skipped, reason **duplicate of `<id>`**. Equal only after one of the 8 turns and/or a mirror → kept, reported **turned copy of `<id>`** (C1's default; the other answer turns this branch into a skip).<br>7. **Write:** each accepted figure becomes `Tangrams/<id>.json`: UTF-8, LF line ends, no BOM, the shipped files' layout (2-space indent, one solution entry and one art shape per line). An existing file is never overwritten (the run stops). It also writes the S-40 lists. The `--report` file (markdown, in the scratch) holds the rows S-41 needs, the skipped table (source id · name · reason), the counts by reason, the turned copies, the titles (en, fi) and the rating distribution. The implementer copies them into the shared files with the Edit/Write tools (the ledger rule). `--dry-run` writes only the report.<br>Also `show <file>`: the bounding box and each piece's corners as floats, an authoring aid for the batches | REQ-038 statement and rules (V1–V6, V8–V10); REQ-007.A1; TYPE-001, TYPE-003; DI-10 and `design-inputs.md` §2 item 16; G-05, G-16 | TASK-036; the batches (`show`) | TASK-036 |
| S-39 | An imported file's fields (`Tangrams/<id>.json`) | - `format` `tangram-puzzle/1`. `kind` **`full`** for every import, so the list still starts with the two minis and the four shipped warm-ups (Letter Q and Letter U pass V12 in the probe and stay `full`; R3).<br>- `category`: convex-13 → `shapes`. Android groups: `base`, `geometric` → `shapes`; `alphabet` → `letters`; `numbers` → `numbers`; `animals` → `animals`; `people` → `people`; `objects`, `Christmas`, `icons` → `things`. By subject instead: `vehicles` ← Boat, Cargo Ship, Helicopter, Plane, Pram, Rocket, Sailboat, Sled, Sleigh; `nature` ← Mountain, Tree, Tree (short), Crescent Moon, Star; `people` ← Caroler, Fairy, Person with Gift, Snowman.<br>- `id` = `<category>-<slug>`. The slug is the source id after its group prefix ("alphabet-A" → `a`, "objects-house" → `house`, "aminals-bird" → `bird`), in lower case, with every run of other characters as one hyphen; convex-13's `convex-NN` keeps its two digits (`shapes-convex-01`). If that id is taken (a shipped id or an earlier import) or has a segment `kit` (the content kit's crafted ids), the slug is the slug of the final `title.en`. The id is unique, kebab-case (the schema's pattern), equal to the file name, and never a shipped id.<br>- `title.en`: android's `name` as it is, except: (a) a title another bundled puzzle already has (case-insensitive; the 13 or an earlier import) gets a distinguishing word taken from the figure; (b) a word unsuitable for young children is replaced (Tobacco Pipe → Pipe); (c) brackets become plain words (Tree (short) → Short tree). Convex-13's "Convex N" become plain, unique shape names. Every `title.en` in the bundle is unique. `title.fi` is a reference translation (G-10; `design-inputs.md` §2 item 9), never tested. Every edit and every fi title go into one `review-notes.md` row.<br>- `difficulty`, REQ-040's rating (C2's default): E is the mean, over the seven pieces, of V12's exposure (the share of a piece's outline on the silhouette's edge, as `tools/validate_puzzles.py` V12 measures it). E ≥ 0.56 → 1; 0.52 ≤ E < 0.56 → 2; 0.47 ≤ E < 0.52 → 3; 0.40 ≤ E < 0.47 → 4; E < 0.40 → 5. The more of every piece shows in the silhouette, the easier (REQ-041's own measure, averaged). The 13 keep their ratings.<br>- `provenance` `{"author": "ai:claude", "reviewedByHuman": false}`: the schema's two fields (owner rule 7). It has one colour, so S-42's report and TASK-041's pin show a picture not yet drawn. **Who writes the shared files** (Q4). A file not in this table is written by nobody in this WO.

| Shared file | Written by | Read by |
|---|---|---|
| `Tangrams/<id>.json`, an import | TASK-036: every field, `art` as the stub. Its batch: the `art` object only | every test that reads the bundle; the app |
| A shipped `Tangrams/*.json` and its committed sheet (SVG, PNG) | nobody (seam note 1) | the same |
| `Tangrams/previews/<id>.svg`, an import's sheet | its batch, by the renderer, one file per invocation | the owner; the orchestrator (through the PNG copy) |
| `ASSET-LICENCES.md` | TASK-036, once and complete | TASK-041's pin; TASK-042 |
| `Tangrams/README.md` (the import section: the counts by reason, the skipped figures with their reasons, the turned copies, a pointer to `ASSET-LICENCES.md`) | TASK-036 | the owner; the G4 surface |
| `review-notes.md` (one row: every imported title, en and fi, and every title edit) | TASK-036. The owner's play-test row is the orchestrator's, at close | the owner |
| `tools/import/batch-1.txt` … `tools/import/batch-4.txt` | TASK-036 | its batch; TASK-042 |
| PNG copies of the sheets | each station, in the scratch folder its brief names; never committed | that station; the orchestrator |

**The ten questions, settled.** R-n is a reading for a `decisions.md` row; C-n a checkpoint item.

1. **Conversion (R1).** S-38 steps 1–4 and 7. The converter lives in `tools/import_figures.py` (Python, stdlib, beside the validator it imports) and stays in the repo. What checks it is REQ-038 through `tools/validate_puzzles.py` (V1–V6, V8–V10) and the Kotlin load check: `PuzzleLibraries.bundled` in the content kit, REQ-038.A1 and REQ-007.A1 on the JVM line. The collection's numbers (its `overlap`, `maxPieceAreaError` and `mirrored`) are never the oracle. `tools/tests/test_import_figures.py` is the implementer's self-check, not a verdict.
2. **What passes and what is a duplicate (R2, C1).** "Passes" means both checks: the validator in S-38 step 5, and the load check on the JVM line in TASK-036's Done-check (4). "The same figure" means equal silhouettes after a translation (S-38 step 6). The shipped 13 win, then convex-13, then android-tangram-131, each in file order. A turned or mirrored copy is kept and flagged (C1). The skipped figures, with reasons and counts, go in `Tangrams/README.md`. Probe: 9 duplicates (the shipped Gift box ← Android's Square; the shipped Chocolate bar ← Convex 3; six of Android's geometric figures ← their convex twins; Letter O ← Number 0). Six turned copies: Convex 9 (the Gift box turned 45°), Convex 13 (the Mountain turned 45°), Parallelogram, Trapezoid, Letter V (the Chevron turned), Letter W (Letter M turned).
3. **The fields (R3, C2).** S-39. A new warm-up would change the start of the list everyone sees, so every import is `full`; nothing at the start changes, and no checkpoint item is needed. If the owner later wants Letter Q and Letter U as warm-ups, that is one field each, and the content kit's shipped-warm-up fixture check then meets them (Q7). The rating rule is C2. Probe distribution for ratings 1…5 (informative): about 23 / 20 / 18 / 10 / 22. The titles go to `review-notes.md`, never to a checkpoint.
4. **The pictures (R4).** Four batches (S-40), `[P]` by the three-part rule (intro). Each picture is drawn for this project (G-16 v1.2), inside the outline (the screen clips: REQ-039 statement, `Spec/03` §2), flat, 3–8 colours, no text (REQ-039 rule 1), in S-42's grammar, following `Spec/03` § Art guidelines.
   - **"Looked at":** after its last change to a file, the batch author opens that file's PNG copy and writes one line: does the silhouette read as its title; is the picture recognisable at the sheet's size, inside the outline, with nothing important at the very edge and no text; its colour count (S-42).
   - **The orchestrator** opens every PNG of a batch before accepting its hand-back (`orchestrator.md`) and may return a picture to its author.
   - **The owner's look** is the review (REQ-039 rule 2, WO-009). `review-notes.md` points at the sheets.
5. **`ASSET-LICENCES.md` (R5).** S-41. TASK-041's JVM pin keeps it complete: every `Tangrams/*.json` has exactly one row, every row names a file, and each figure has its licence. A review check would not be enough: the pin runs on every later JVM line, so a puzzle added later without its row turns the build red. Listing the 13 makes "every file has a row" decidable without a source field the locked schema forbids.
6. **Start-up and the grid (R6).** Measured by S-43 and three cold starts: the 13-puzzle baseline on all three images (TASK-035); early, on the floor, on the new geometry with stub pictures (TASK-036); and the record on the final bundle on all three images (TASK-042).
   - **Start-up threshold:** the median launch-to-first-puzzle is above **5 s** on any image (Android vitals counts a cold start of 5 s or more as excessive).
   - **Design if crossed** (proposed; a `decisions.md` row and a task with its REQ, never a silent change): `:content` parses on the device and the full validity check runs at build time only. The JVM kit's `bundled(...)` over the same files already fails the build on a bad file (`decisions.md` 2026-10-11 "A bundled puzzle that fails a rule fails fast"), so the `IPuzzleLibrary` KDoc's "never list a puzzle that fails the validity rules" stays true. Its REQs: REQ-007.A1 and REQ-038.
   - **Grid threshold:** the median `gridOpen` is above **1 s** on any image.
   - **Design if crossed:** a lazy grid, which reverses "all composed" in `decisions.md` 2026-10-15 "The grid overview, as drawn" (a new row). It is a `:play` task on REQ-050 plus kit corrections, because the overview kits read every cell node.
   - **Scrolling** is recorded, not judged: the emulators draw in software (`swiftshader_indirect`). It goes on the owner's play-test list for a real phone.
   - A crossing at TASK-036 is a `recirculate → planner` with the numbers. The batches do not depend on it.
   - *Informative arithmetic, never an oracle:* the widest imports (Boat, Bridge, Cargo Ship, about 9.9 units wide) draw about 28 dp per unit on a 360 dp phone, so their pieces are small. That is a line for the owner's play-test, not a REQ breach.
7. **The tests that read the bundle (R7).** The table below. A quantifier stays "every". The two changed tests land before (TASK-033) or with (TASK-036) the files that would break them. Device run time grows about 8× for the per-puzzle classes and about 66× for REQ-003.A1's "from any puzzle to every other" test. The play set takes about 2–2.5 h per image, the app set about 1 h.
8. **The prototype and the generated outputs (R4).** `tools/build_prototype.py` (DI-1) and `tools/make_ui_sheets.py` (DI-2) are not run, and `tools/tests/test_prototype.py` is not run either: it needs Playwright and serves no REQ of this WO. This follows the precedent of `decisions.md` 2026-10-11 "The `outline_corners` fix does not regenerate the prototype or the UI sheets". G-07 names only the requirements and puzzle validators, and DI-1 is never DoD. Their next regeneration picks up the imports.
   - **Sheets:** the imports' sheets only, one file per invocation into `Tangrams/previews/`. The 13 committed sheets are not re-rendered: a re-render rewrites their line ends and drops their C2PA metadata, with the drawing unchanged (run log g3-py). The whole-folder render goes to the scratch.
   - **PNG copies:** in the scratch, never committed.
9. **Coverage and channels (R8).** The table below. The existing tokens prove all eleven lines over the new bundle, and trace-check `--wo WO-010` stays GREEN (11/11) with no token moved.
   - **REQ-039.A1's honest oracle** at this size is still WO-003's drawing test over every bundled puzzle, `SolvedPictureKitTest`'s "no picture paints outside its puzzle's outline" on the floor and dev images. The line is about what the screen paints, and the screen clips. A JVM or tool check of the art data would prove a different, stronger property (shapes inside the outline) and miss a lost clip.
   - **The colour rule** (REQ-039 rule 1) gets an untokened JVM pin over the imports (TASK-041).
   - Each row names its task's channel.
10. **The counter's digits (R9).** The carry is kept, as S-11Δ2: TASK-033's untokened JVM pin, TASK-034's one-line fix. It serves REQ-047's statement ("in English otherwise"): on an Arabic or Persian device the app is English, and an English counter has ASCII digits. REQ-024.A2's number is the counter's. Dropping it would leave a defect against a locked statement for one line of code.

## WO-008 — #PlayTime: active play time, puzzle time and best time, the timer on the board  (Mode: Full (b))

Mode **Full (b)** (`workorders/WO-008.md` § Mode). The reason is a new governed interface: `IActiveTime` in `:contracts`, implemented in `:kernel` (O-04, G-12).

The design is `designs/WO-008-design.md` (Reviewed; the review's F1, F2 (a), F4 and F5 are written back). Its §2 seam table (S-44 … S-48 and the Δ rows) is this plan's seam table, and each row there names its task. This section adds the planner's seam notes, one planner seam (S-37Δ), the file names the design leaves open, and the coverage table. Briefs: `workorders/WO-008-briefs.md`.

Rows are in landing order. The task numbers are the design's, which `architecture.md` v1.4 and the WO's Contract deltas already cite, so they do not run in ascending order.

- **Where this cut differs from the design's §4.**
  - **Each kit stage lands with or before the product change it pins** (`decisions.md` 2026-10-20 "Every test edit in WO-008 is the acceptance-test author's …"):
    - TASK-045 (the JVM kit) lands before TASK-044 and TASK-046;
    - TASK-048 lands in one step with TASK-047;
    - TASK-050 (the `app/` lines) lands before TASK-049.
  - **No `[P]`.** The design paired TASK-045 ∥ TASK-046. That pair does not hold: the controller kit calls S-1Δ3, S-2Δ3 and S-19Δ, so it compiles only against TASK-046's seams, and a kit that pins TASK-046 lands before it. The author drafts beside the implementers; that is dispatch, not a plan dependency.
  - **REQ-031's device kit moves into TASK-048**, which lands with the timer it pins (TASK-047). The design's TASK-050 keeps the `app/` lines.
  - **TASK-051 is new.** It runs every channel once, on the final tree.
- **Lanes.**
  - **Test lane.** One `acceptance-test-author`, its context resumed between stages:
    - TASK-045, stage 1: the JVM kit, and the in-tree corrections that a player's best time forces;
    - TASK-048, stage 2: the F4 edits, the format pin and the timer's device kit;
    - TASK-050, stage 3: the `app/` device lines and the death pair's times.
  - **Implementer lane:** TASK-043 → TASK-044 → TASK-046 → TASK-047 → TASK-049 → TASK-051.
  - **Hardest first.**
    - TASK-043 goes first because the WO says so: it moves the floor under every device verdict.
    - Among the product tasks, TASK-046 carries the design's highest-risk part: the fold-and-save order, whose mistakes are silent on screen. It comes right after TASK-044, the interface it needs.
- **Task class** (staffing, orchestration §2; each Description starts with it). Every row goes to the strongest model; none is mechanical.
  - Device-only: TASK-043, TASK-049, TASK-051.
  - Concurrency: TASK-046.
  - UI and rendering: TASK-047.
  - Kit stages with Compose or device legs: TASK-048, TASK-050.
  - JVM oracles of the highest-risk part: TASK-045.
  - A governed interface and time arithmetic with must-nevers: TASK-044.
- **Red windows.** Gradle compiles each source set as a whole. Nobody runs a set for a verdict inside its window; inside one, the briefs' JVM line leaves the red module out.
  - **`kernel/src/test`:** from TASK-045's landing until TASK-044 lands (S-44 and S-45 are unresolved).
  - **`play/src/test`:** from TASK-045's landing until TASK-046 lands (S-44 and the S-1Δ3, S-2Δ3 and S-19Δ names are unresolved).
  - **`play/src/test` and `play/src/androidTest` at the TASK-048 / TASK-047 step:** no window. The two land in one step, because F4's `hoursMinutes` has no default.
  - **`app/src/androidTest`:** from TASK-050's landing until TASK-049 lands (S-46 `AppClock` is unresolved).
- **Seam notes** (the planner's; binding like the rows).
  1. **Every existing construction compiles unchanged** (design S-1Δ3 and S-19Δ; the review's "Constructor overloads"). A controller built without a clock (`time` null) counts nothing, so every WO-003 … WO-010 kit keeps its timings. The only compile breaks are F4's five files (TASK-048).
  2. **A player's solve now sets the best time** (S-2Δ3).
     - Without a clock, that is the puzzle's loaded time: 0 for one never counted.
     - An in-tree assertion that a player's solve leaves "–", or `bestSeconds` null, pinned WO-004's interim (`decisions.md` 2026-10-15 "The top bar, Restart and the solved row, as drawn": "until WO-008 computes times"). TASK-045 corrects each one before TASK-046 lands.
     - The play-through's closed world of texts admits the solved row's best time (`decisions.md` 2026-10-20 "The play-through's closed world of texts admits the best time …").
     - The aid's tripwires keep their meaning (REQ-046.A3 in `DevAidKitTest` and `DevAidScreenKitTest`; must-do 1): an aid solve withholds the best (S-27Δ).
  3. **The kit's clock moves, it never sleeps** (design §2 "Kit doors").
     - **JVM:** a kit `Clock`, the real `ActiveTime`, and `countTime()` after each jump.
     - **`play/` device:** the same, with the kit calling `time.touched()`.
     - **`app/` device:** `AppClock.override`, and touches injected through the window (`Instrumentation.sendPointerSync` or `UiAutomation.injectInputEvent`). Never `performTouchInput` or `performClick`: they skip `MainActivity.dispatchTouchEvent` (design library fact 7; review F1).
     - Every "at most" or "nothing" line has its positive control in the same test.
  4. **No `app/` kit class writes the timer setting** (`decisions.md` 2026-10-20 "REQ-031.A2 is proven in `play/` only …").
     - `AppGraph` builds one controller per process, over one cached `Persistence`. A store write made after that never reaches it, and one made before it would show the timer in every later class of the run.
     - `AppClock.override` is reset in `finally`. That backwards jump is S-45's F5 case.
  5. **Who edits tests.** Every test edit in WO-008 is the acceptance-test author's, scaffolding tests included. An implementer whose change breaks a test hands back `recirculate → acceptance-test-author`, naming the file and the line.
  6. **The floor** (TASK-043).
     - `minSdk 31` goes into the four Android modules.
     - No product `SDK_INT` check exists (grep, 2026-10-20). The always-true branches stay (`decisions.md` 2026-10-20 "`minSdk 31` leaves the always-true API branches in place …").
     - Every `platform-notes/android.md` rule marked as API 26 history is re-judged on `Phone_API_31`.
- **In-tree tests this WO changes** (all by the acceptance-test author):
  - **TASK-045:** `KitApp.kt`'s `closedWorld()` and `languageWorld()` (app device kit), and every assertion its grep finds that a player's solve leaves no best time.
  - **TASK-048:** F4's five files.
    - `hoursMinutes` in the `PlayTexts` literals: `KitScreen.kt:114`, `CounterDigitsKitTest.kt:38,46`, `DecorationScaffoldTest.kt:56`, `ScreenSeamScaffoldTest.kt:74`.
    - `ControlsScaffoldTest.kt:116–119`: the new `bestTimeText(seconds, texts)`, and "60:00" becomes "1 h 0 min" (a corrected check).
  - **TASK-050:** `ProcessDeathKitTest.kt`'s two phases and `KitApp.kt`'s helpers.
  - **Not a test:** `tools/device_reset.sh` (TASK-043, S-37Δ).
- **Lines covered twice, on purpose:**
  - REQ-029.A1: in `kernel/` (the code home), and in `app/` through the window (review F1).
  - REQ-031.A1: in `play/` (the code home: an empty store), and in `app/` (a cleared app).
  - REQ-005.A2: in `play/`, and on the death pair's `phase2` in `app/`.
- **Unverified by design:** none. REQ-005.A1 and A2 are verified as locked, at the seam (`decisions.md` 2026-10-20 "REQ-005's two lines are verified as locked, at the seam …"). `capture-outbox/chg-draft-req005-available.md` stays pending; its screen half is WO-007's.
- **The must-dos.**
  1. **An aid solve never sets a best time:** S-2Δ3 and S-27Δ in TASK-046, pinned by TASK-045 and by the REQ-046.A3 tripwires.
  2. **No time the store's reader rejects is ever written:** S-45 in TASK-044 (`decisions.md` 2026-10-20 "No time the store's reader rejects is ever written …" and its F5 amendment), pinned by TASK-045.
  3. **O-04:** the transitions stay in `PlayController` (design §1; `decisions.md` 2026-10-20 "O-04 read for WO-008 …" and its amendment). TASK-046 builds on that.
- **Contract deltas** (the WO's § Contract deltas).
  - TASK-044 writes `IActiveTime.kt` (new) and rewords lines 19–20 of the `IProgressStore` KDoc. Both files are `contract_paths.notify`, so each edit makes a `contract-delta` event.
  - The orchestrator fills the two rows and re-freezes the baseline once TASK-044 is accepted.
  - No other contract file changes.
- **Verification channels (G3).** The commands are in the briefs' channel table.
  - **Python tool channel:** `tools/validate_puzzles.py Tangrams` (106/106; no puzzle changes) and `validate.py` on `Requirements/` (0 errors).
  - **JVM:** the two-run line, all six modules.
  - **Device:**
    - the floor image `Phone_API_31` first, then the dev image `Medium_Phone_API_37.0`, then the tablet image `Medium_Tablet_API_37.0`, one emulator at a time;
    - one background script per image: boot detached, reset + `--check`, the classes one per invocation, reset + `--check`, kill;
    - each run judged by its XML, under the 40-minute no-test-finished watchdog;
    - the play set on the floor and dev images, the app set on all three (WO-006 practice: the play set hosts `PlayScreen` at the kit's phone size).
  - **The process-death pair** (`tools/process_death_check.sh`, ADR-003's row-11 harness; it carries the death pair's times): on the floor and dev images. The WO's channel list does not name it. Its two images are proven (run log g3-floor, g3-dev), and the harness is unchanged since WO-004.
  - **Release:** V-01 and its positive control.
  - **Lint, once (TASK-043):** `ObsoleteSdkInt` in product code.
  - **Device time** (informative; from run log t042c):
    - about 3–3.5 h at TASK-043 (every class alone, three images);
    - about 2 h 50 min at TASK-051 (the whole sets);
    - about 1.5 h across TASK-046, TASK-047 and TASK-049;
    - about 7–8 h in all.
  - **Not DoD channels:** human eyes (the owner plays at close: the timer, the best time after a solve, the "1 h 5 min" form; `review-notes.md`). Browser: none.
- **Not covered here** (no token):
  - REQ-029.A2, and REQ-030 rule 3's settings list → WO-007, with the screen (`decisions.md` 2026-10-20 "WO-008 #PlayTime runs before WO-007 #Settings …").
  - The carried optional kit item (the other build files as staleness inputs of `ReleasePromiseKitTest`) stays carried. The JVM line's run 1 forces both package tasks, so no stale APK reaches a verdict in this WO.

### Seams (the planner's, beside the design's seam table)

S-1 … S-43 are the earlier sections' (with `designs/WO-004-design.md` §2). S-44 … S-48 and WO-008's Δ rows are `designs/WO-008-design.md` §2's. A seam that must change during P4 is a `recirculate → planner` and a design write-back, never a quiet edit.

## WO-007 — #Settings: the settings screen, the timer and sound switches, sounds and the haptic tick, the play times, reset  (Mode: Full (b))

Mode **Full (b)** (`workorders/WO-007.md` § Mode): a governed interface changes — `IActiveTime` gains `resetPlayTimes()` (notify; `architecture.md` v1.5). Format 1 already holds the settings and the play times, so no progress-file field is added: no reason (c), no hard-stop 2.

The design is `designs/WO-007-design.md` (Reviewed; F1–F5 written back; F1 re-checked and resolved in `reviews/WO-007-design-review.md`). Its §2 seam table (S-44Δ, S-45Δ, S-19Δ2, S-1Δ4, S-2Δ4, S-11Δ4, S-53, S-22Δ3, S-49 with its tags and strings, S-50, S-51, S-52, S-15Δ4) is this plan's seam table, and each row there names its task. **No seam changes at planning.** This section adds the planner's seam notes, the file names (§ Seams, the "Files" row), the in-tree tests the WO moves and the coverage table. Briefs: `workorders/WO-007-briefs.md`.

Rows are in landing order. The task numbers are the design's (`architecture.md` v1.5 and the WO's Contract deltas cite S-44Δ's TASK-053), so TASK-058 comes before TASK-057.

- **Where this cut differs from the design's §4** (`decisions.md` 2026-10-21 "The WO-007 kit lands in four stages …" and "F5's second-line pin is an app-kit class …"):
  - **`Feedback`'s JVM pins move from TASK-052 to TASK-060** (kit stage 4). In stage 1 they would hold `app/src/test` in a compile window from the first landing until TASK-061, the last product task, and `ReleasePromiseKitTest` (REQ-010.A2's JVM line) lives in that source set.
  - **The sounds' pin ("each WAV ≤ 1 s, and each one listed") moves from TASK-052 to TASK-058** (kit stage 3), which **lands in one step with TASK-057**. It reads `ASSET-LICENCES.md` and the five WAVs TASK-057 writes: before them it could not be green, after them the asset would land ahead of its oracle.
  - **No `[P]`.** The design's TASK-057 ∥ TASK-056 shares a declared input: both change sources of the release APK (`app/src/main/res/raw`, `play/src/main`), and both Done-checks run the JVM line, whose `ReleasePromiseKitTest` and `ReleaseBuildKitTest` read that APK (`task-decomposition.md`, the v0.18 `[P]` rule).
  - **F5's pin is an app-kit class** (`TopBarFitKitTest`) in kit stage 2, still in one step with S-22Δ3: its `fi` leg needs `:app`'s resources, which the play kit cannot read.
  - **The death pair** runs on the floor and dev at TASK-054, TASK-059, TASK-061 and TASK-062 (`decisions.md` 2026-10-21 "The death pair carries no new WO-007 line …"). TASK-059 runs every `KitApp` class stage 3 reaches alone on the floor and dev (its amendment, "Amends … TASK-059 runs every `KitApp` class alone …"), and TASK-061 runs the four `KitApp`-only classes alone there again (the second amendment, "Amends … TASK-061 runs the four `KitApp` classes alone too …").
- **Lanes.**
  - **Test lane.** One `acceptance-test-author`, its context resumed between stages:
    - TASK-052, stage 1: the JVM oracles of the reset order, the settings state and the pause;
    - TASK-055, stage 2: the gear pins, the moved-› corrections, F5's pin;
    - TASK-058, stage 3: the `app/` device kit of the screen and the reset, the every-screen extensions, the kit doors, the sounds' file pin;
    - TASK-060, stage 4: REQ-033.A1's door on the device, and `Feedback`'s JVM pins.
  - **Implementer lane:** TASK-053 → TASK-054 → TASK-056 → TASK-057 → TASK-059 → TASK-061 → TASK-062.
  - **One-step landings:** TASK-055 with TASK-056; TASK-058 with TASK-057 (the briefs' "How the one-step landings run").
  - **Hardest first.** TASK-054 carries the design's highest-risk part, `resetAll`'s order (silent until a restart); it comes right after TASK-053, the interface member it calls. REQ-033.A1's oracle (the second risk) presses the real sound switch, so S-50 and S-51 come after the screen (S-49).
- **Task class** (staffing, orchestration §2; each Description starts with it). Every row goes to the strongest model; `slice-implementer` and `acceptance-test-author` run on Opus in this project, and a station is never lowered (`decisions.md` 2026-10-21 "Amends … TASK-059 runs every `KitApp` class alone …").
  - Concurrency and persistence order: TASK-054.
  - A governed interface and time arithmetic with must-nevers: TASK-053.
  - UI and rendering: TASK-056, TASK-059.
  - Device-only: TASK-061, TASK-062.
  - Kit stages: TASK-052 (JVM oracles of the highest-risk part); TASK-055, TASK-058, TASK-060 (Compose and device legs).
  - Mechanical, run on the station's Opus: TASK-057 (a deterministic stdlib generator from fixed recipes, five files, one ledger section).
- **Red windows.** Gradle compiles each source set as a whole. Nobody runs a set for a verdict inside its window; inside one, run 2 of the JVM line leaves the red module out.
  - **`kernel/src/test`:** from TASK-052's landing until TASK-053 lands (S-44Δ's member is unresolved).
  - **`play/src/test`:** from TASK-052's landing until TASK-054 lands (S-1Δ4, S-2Δ4 and S-19Δ2's names are unresolved).

So it plays no click (design library fact 2), and `TouchTargetKitTest`'s completeness check does not see it until stage 3 lists it. Between TASK-056 and TASK-059 a gear press sets `settingsOpen` with nothing on screen; only the play kit's gear pins press it then, on their own controller.

They extend `tasks.md` § WO-006 seam note 3: every app-kit class leaves the controller, the settings and the device as it found them; a class that resets leaves every puzzle New.

## WO-009 — #Release: the marked release set, the mark command, the gate on the store build, the launcher and store icons, the store kit and the owner's checklist  (Mode: Light)

Mode **Light** (`workorders/WO-009.md` § Mode): none of the four reasons holds. The mark command and the picture tools are tools in `tools/`, the store kit is a folder of text and pictures, the icon resources live in `:app`; `IPuzzleLibrary` and the `tangram-puzzle/1` format are consumed as locked and `Types.kt` as frozen; saved progress is keyed on ids, so a release with fewer puzzles changes no format; signing, accounts and money stay out. There is no design: § Seams below is the design, as in § WO-010. It settles the WO's eleven questions. Each reading no REQ fixes goes to the orchestrator as a planner reading R1–R13 (titles proposed in the hand-back; the briefs cite them by those titles); the orchestrator writes the `decisions.md` rows. The product-visible readings fall under the WO's five checkpoint items. Briefs: `workorders/WO-009-briefs.md`. Rows are in landing order.

- **Lanes.**
  - **Test lane** (one `acceptance-test-author`, its context resumed between stages): TASK-064 (stage 1, JVM: the release bundle's marks and volume, REQ-048 A3's tokens) lands before TASK-065; TASK-066 (stage 2, device: the installed launcher icon, REQ-002 A2 on every player screen) lands before TASK-067; TASK-069 (stage 3, JVM pins over files: the store icon, the listing drafts, the privacy page, the console answers, the banner) lands after TASK-067 and TASK-068, whose files it reads.
  - **Implementer lane:** TASK-063 (the mark command) → TASK-065 (the release set, the gate, the build's identity lines) → TASK-067 (the icons, the manifest lines, `ASSET-LICENCES.md`, the release install leg; device) → TASK-068 (the store kit) → TASK-070 (every channel on the reviewed tree, before the owner's marks) → *the owner's marks* → TASK-071 (after the marks: the release set green, the store artifact, the release APK on a device, the screenshots).
  - **The mark command first** (WO question 2): the owner can mark from TASK-063's landing while the rest is built (checkpoint item 1).
  - **Hardest first** after it: TASK-065 carries the WO's product risk. It is a per-build-type resource set under AGP 9.4's variant API that must leave the debug bundle (every JVM and device kit's input) exactly as it is, and a gate that stops the AAB but never `assembleRelease`.
  - The author's drafting beside other tasks is dispatch, not a plan dependency.
- **Task class** (staffing, orchestration §2; each Description starts with it). Every station runs on Opus in this project.
  - Mechanical, on the station's Opus: TASK-063 (a stdlib tool), TASK-068 (text files and one picture tool).
  - Strongest model: TASK-065 (build logic), TASK-067 (rendering, resources, device legs), TASK-070 and TASK-071 (device-only).
  - Kit stages: TASK-064 (JVM), TASK-066 (Compose device legs and pixel sampling), TASK-069 (JVM pins over files).
- **No `[P]`.** Every pair shares a declared input: the bundle `Tangrams/*.json` (TASK-063's `status`, every kit through `:content`, TASK-065's filter), the two APKs (every JVM line), or the release APK's sources (TASK-065's build files, TASK-067's manifest and resources).
- **Red windows and the expected red.** Nobody runs a set for a verdict inside a window.
  - **The expected red, until the owner's marks:** `ReleaseSetKitTest`'s legs over the release bundle (REQ-042 A1–A3 and the release-set pins) are red from TASK-065's landing until the owner has marked a release-ready set (at TASK-064's landing they read the unfiltered APK). Each failure names its count. The class's crafted-fixture legs are green throughout. **Run 2 of the JVM line therefore runs with `--continue` during WO-009 and is judged by the XMLs:** a task's "the JVM line green" means every test but those legs.
  - **`play/src/test`, one leg:** `ReleaseMarksKitTest`'s REQ-039 A2 leg is red from TASK-064's landing (the release APK still carries all 106 unmarked files: the in-situ fixture) until TASK-065 lands.
  - **`app/src/androidTest`, one class:** `LauncherIconKitTest` fails at run time from TASK-066's landing (the installed app still shows the platform's default icon) until TASK-067 lands.
  - **No compile window:** no kit file names a symbol or resource id an implementer adds (seam note 3).
- **Seam notes** (the planner's; binding like the rows).
  1. **Released means shipped.** The release bundle is chosen at build time in `:content` (S-55): an unmarked puzzle file is absent from the release APK and the AAB, so REQ-039 A2 and REQ-042 are read from the release APK's files (R4). The debug build keeps every file; the owner's children play it.
  2. **The marks change one field.** The mark command changes only `"reviewedByHuman": false` ↔ `true` in the named files (S-54). No main code path of the debug build reads the field (`PuzzleDefinition.reviewedByHuman` has no reader outside `:content`'s parser; grep 2026-10-22). So the owner's marks change the release bundle only, and the device sets run once, on the pre-marks tree (R12). TASK-071 checks the marks' diff before relying on this.
  3. **The kit names what it reads by path, or by what the platform resolves.** The launcher icon is read through `PackageManager` for the launcher activity, the store files by path. No kit file names a resource id or symbol an implementer adds, so every stage compiles at its landing.
  4. **No token for the owner-evidence lines.** REQ-001.A2, REQ-048.A1 and REQ-048.A2 are proven by the owner's signed `owner-evidence.md` rows. trace-check counts a test token before an owner row, so no test file carries those dotted IDs, and test prose writes them with a space ("REQ-048 A2"). TASK-069's pins over the drafts carry no token.
  5. **The run log's trees grow** (`.swdev/run-log/WO-009.md` header). The product tree adds `release/`, `PRIVACY.md` and `Spec/icon/` (read by the icon tool and the pins). The tools tree adds `tools/release_install.sh` and `tools/store_screenshots.sh` (`tools/*.py` already holds the new Python tools, and `tools/tests/` their tests). The orchestrator extends the definition before the first row that needs it. `Tangrams/README.md` (TASK-063) and `review-notes.md` (TASK-068, TASK-071) stay deliberately unhashed: they are documents, and no test reads them.
  6. **Who edits tests.** Kit files are the author's, by stage. The tool tests `tools/tests/test_mark_puzzles.py` and `tools/tests/test_make_icon.py` are the implementers'. An implementer whose change breaks any other test hands back `recirculate → acceptance-test-author`, naming the file and the line.
  7. **Hard limits** (the owner's and the framework's).
     - No station sets `reviewedByHuman: true`, runs `mark` or `unmark` on `Tangrams/`, or edits a puzzle file (owner rule 7, G-05). The mark command runs on copies only.
     - No signing config, key, keystore, secret, account or console entry (hard-stop 1, F2). The only signature is the throwaway debug-key copy in a station's scratch (S-62).
     - `IPuzzleLibrary.kt` and `Tangrams/puzzle.schema.json` are never edited. No permission, network, ad or purchase code (G-01). No new window (code review N3 of WO-007 stays a note).
- **In-tree tests this WO touches** (the planner's grep, 2026-10-22, for tests that read the release APK, the bundle, `ASSET-LICENCES.md` or the "every screen" closed world):
  - **Edited by the author** (token edits change no assertion):
    - `app/src/test/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/app/ReleasePromiseKitTest.kt`: `theReleaseApkDeclaresNoPermission` gains REQ-048 A3's token beside REQ-010 A2's (TASK-064).
    - `app/src/androidTest/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/app/PlayThroughKitTest.kt`: `theInstalledAppDeclaresNoPermissionsNotEvenNetworkAccess`, the same (TASK-064).
    - `app/src/androidTest/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/app/SettingsKitTest.kt`: REQ-002 A2's settings half. `noDifficultyControlIsPresent` already asserts the screen's exact control set; the token goes there, or on a new leg (the author's call; TASK-066).
  - **Read, and green without an edit:**
    - `play/src/test/kotlin/app/tangramplaytime/play/ReleaseBuildKitTest.kt` and V-01: the release APK's DEV-aid markers, permissions, canary and `okhttp3`. Fewer puzzle files touch none of them.
    - `ReleasePromiseKitTest.kt`'s dex allow-list: icons and a manifest line add no class.
    - The content kit (`PuzzleLibraryKitTest.kt`, whose REQ-042 A2 and A3 tokens over the whole library stay WO-002's; `PuzzleLoaderKitTest.kt`; `ContentScaffoldTest.kt`) and every play and app kit: the debug bundle keeps all 106 (S-55).
    - `AssetLicencesKitTest.kt` and `SoundAssetsKitTest.kt`: TASK-067's new `## Launcher icon` section comes after `## Sounds`. Each test reads its section up to the next `## ` heading, and the convex-13 Credit cell stays non-empty.
    - `KitApp.kt`'s closed world (`:app`'s R strings as a whole), `TouchTargetKitTest.kt`'s clickable list and `LanguageKitTest.kt`: no string, no control and no window is added.
    - `tools/tests/test_import_figures.py` `test_layout_is_the_shipped_layout` re-formats four shipped files. A mark keeps the layout, so it stays green after the owner's marks (a cross-check of S-54's byte rule). `tools/tests/test_tangram_geom.py` pins corners only.
- **Pending drafts read for this scope** (the build follows the Contract as locked):
  - `chg-draft-req048-store-channel.md`: Google Play; the closed test is a conditional step of the checklist.
  - `chg-draft-req048-console-declarations-acceptance.md`: A5–A8 are not in scope; the console answers file covers the declarations they would prove.
  - `chg-draft-licence-notice-and-assets.md`: the asset rule G-16 follows; the licence line stays built and untokened.
  - `chg-draft-req042-themes-named.md`, `chg-draft-acceptance-content-ids.md`: REQ-042 A2 and A3 as locked (the categories of `Spec/03-puzzle-format.md`; `shapes-square` by id).
  - `chg-draft-req047-english-contract.md`, `chg-draft-req009-req049-finnish-wording.md`: the listing's and the privacy page's Finnish are reference texts.
  - `chg-draft-depends-fields-complete.md`: REQ-042 → REQ-039; REQ-048 → REQ-001, REQ-009, REQ-010, REQ-049. No ordering change.
  - **Others that touch this scope:** `chg-draft-req046-android-build-terms.md` (a closed-test upload is a release build: G-08 holds for the AAB, which is the same release variant V-01 reads); `chg-draft-req002-a1-mirror.md` (REQ-002 A1 only); `src-draft-g1-owner-answers.md` item 2 (a six-year-old tester: checkpoint item 4's age groups); `src-draft-wo010-checkpoint-answers.md` ("We worry limiting launch ones later": checkpoint item 1, no cap).
- **Verification channels (G3).** The commands are in the briefs' channel table.
  - **Python tool channel:** the validator over `Tangrams` (106/106); `validate.py` on `Requirements/` (0 errors); `tools/tests/test_mark_puzzles.py`, `tools/tests/test_make_icon.py`, `tools/tests/test_tangram_geom.py`, `tools/tests/test_import_figures.py`; `tools/mark_puzzles.py status` (read-only); the two picture tools, run twice for identical bytes.
  - **JVM:** the two-run line; run 2 with `--continue` during WO-009 (the expected red above).
  - **The store artifact:** `:app:bundleRelease` refuses before the owner's marks with S-56's message, and builds after them.
  - **Device:** the floor image `Phone_API_31` first, then the dev image `Medium_Phone_API_37.0`, then the tablet image `Medium_Tablet_API_37.0`; one emulator at a time, one background script per image. Each new class runs alone on the floor and dev (and the tablet for app classes) as it lands (TASK-067); the whole sets run once, on the reviewed pre-marks tree (TASK-070). The death pair is not a WO-009 channel: no persistence or lifecycle change.
  - **The release APK on a device** (S-62): a throwaway copy signed with the local debug key in the station's scratch. It is installed on the floor (and dev at TASK-067) and its permissions are read back (TASK-067, TASK-070). It is launched only after the owner's marks (TASK-071).
  - **Release verifier:** V-01 and its positive control; `run_verifiers.py --wo WO-009` (no new verifier).
  - **Eyes (the AI's):** the store icon, the banner and each screenshot, opened as PNGs. The orchestrator opens each before accepting the hand-back.
  - **Browser:** headless Chrome as the banner's renderer only.
  - **Human eyes (the owner's):** his marks (REQ-039 rule 2); the live listing (the three owner-evidence rows); the `review-notes.md` items (the listing texts, the privacy page, the icons and pictures).
  - **Device time** (informative; per-class times from `.swdev/run-log/WO-007.md` t059–t062): about 55 min at TASK-067, about 3 h 20 min at TASK-070, about 30 min at TASK-071; **about 4 h 45 min in all**, fix loops excluded. `NoToddlerModeKitTest` is unmeasured: TASK-067 times it on the floor first (the WO-010 lesson).
- **Not covered here** (no token): REQ-038 A2 (outside the `--wo` scope, as in WO-002 and WO-010); the licence line (an untokened pin in `SettingsKitTest`); REQ-048 rules 5–7's console answers (owner steps; their acceptance lines are the pending A5–A8 draft); a monochrome icon layer, R8 and trimmed library locales (no REQ asks).

### Seams (the planner's, in place of a design seam table)

S-1 … S-53 are the earlier sections' (with `designs/WO-004-design.md`, `designs/WO-007-design.md` and `designs/WO-008-design.md` §2). The rows below are WO-009's. A seam that must change during P4 is a `recirculate → planner`, never a quiet edit.

| # | Seam (file) | Shape | REQ | Called by | Delivered by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-54 | The mark command (`tools/mark_puzzles.py`, new; its test `tools/tests/test_mark_puzzles.py`, new) | Python 3, stdlib only. `--folder <dir>` (default: the repository's `Tangrams/`, found from the script's own location). The puzzle files are the folder's top-level `*.json` but `*.schema.json`.<br>**`status [--list]`** (read-only; exit 0):<br>- one line per category, in `Spec/03-puzzle-format.md`'s order: `<category>: <marked> of <total> marked`;<br>- then the release bar, one line each, in S-56's words: marked puzzles `N of 20`; categories with a marked puzzle `k of 4` (named); `shapes-square` marked or not; a marked `mini` with exactly three `solution` entries and `difficulty` 1, or none; marked `warmup` puzzles of `difficulty` 1, `w of 4`;<br>- then `release-ready: yes`, or `release-ready: no — <every unmet item>`;<br>- `--list` adds the marked ids, sorted.<br>**`mark <id> [<id> …]` and `unmark <id> [<id> …]`** (exit 0; exit 2 with nothing written):<br>- Every id is checked before any write: the file `<id>.json` exists, parses as JSON, and holds `provenance.reviewedByHuman` as a boolean. An unknown id or a missing field refuses the whole call.<br>- In each file not already in the wanted state, the only change is the one occurrence of `"reviewedByHuman"`, its colon and spacing kept, followed by `false` → `true` (mark) or `true` → `false` (unmark). The tool re-parses the new text, checks that it equals the old object with only that boolean flipped, and writes the bytes: UTF-8, LF, no BOM, the final newline as it was. Both layouts in the folder work: the multi-line `provenance` object and the two minis' one-line one.<br>- It prints `marked <id> — <title.en>` (or `unmarked`, `already marked`, `already unmarked`) per id, then the bar lines.<br>The validator, the renderer, the previews and `ASSET-LICENCES.md` do not read the field, so a mark needs no re-render (G-06).<br>Only the owner runs `mark` or `unmark` on `Tangrams/` (owner rule 7, G-05). Stations run `status` there, and `mark` only on copies (the tool test; TASK-065's positive control).<br>`Tangrams/README.md` gains a short "Marking a puzzle for release" paragraph: the three commands and the bar | REQ-039 rule 2 and A2; REQ-042 statement, rules, A1–A3; REQ-045 statement and rule 3; REQ-041 statement; DI-8 ("A mark command (Recommended)"); owner rule 7; G-05 | the owner; TASK-065 (on a copy); TASK-070, TASK-071 (`status`) | TASK-063 |
| S-55 | The release set (`content/build.gradle.kts`) | Two registrations of the `BundlePuzzles` task class. Each keeps today's output layout (`app/tangramplaytime/content/puzzles/<file>` and the sorted `index.txt`, under `BundledPuzzles.DIR`) in its own output folder:<br>- the **debug bundle:** every top-level `Tangrams/*.json` but the schema, as today;<br>- the **release bundle:** those of them whose `provenance.reviewedByHuman` is JSON `true`. A missing `provenance` or field reads `false` (`decisions.md` 2026-10-11 "A missing `provenance` or `reviewedByHuman` reads as `false`."). The JSON is read with Gradle's own parser (no new dependency). The inputs are content-sensitive, so a mark rebuilds the release bundle.<br>`androidComponents.onVariants` wires the `release` build type to the release bundle and every other build type to the debug bundle. The debug variant's unit-test and device-test classpaths keep all 106 files.<br>Nothing else changes: `BundledPuzzles`, `PuzzleLibraries`, `IPuzzleLibrary.kt` and the schema are untouched. An empty release bundle fails at start with the existing "no puzzle file to list" (`decisions.md` 2026-10-11 "A bundled puzzle that fails a rule fails fast."), so the release APK is never launched before the owner's marks. `:content` stays the only module that reads `Tangrams/*.json` (O-02) | REQ-039 rule 2 and A2; REQ-042; REQ-007 statement; O-02, G-05, G-12 | `:app`'s release APK and AAB; S-56; the kit's release readers (TASK-064) | TASK-065 |
| S-56 | The release-readiness gate (`content/build.gradle.kts` task `checkReleasePuzzles`; `app/build.gradle.kts`, the wiring) | `:content:checkReleasePuzzles` reads S-55's release bundle. It fails unless all five hold, naming every unmet item with its count and pointing at `python tools/mark_puzzles.py status`:<br>1. at least 20 files (REQ-042 A1, rule 1; each is marked, by S-55);<br>2. at least four distinct `category` values (REQ-042 A2, rule 2: the categories of `Spec/03-puzzle-format.md`);<br>3. `shapes-square.json` among them (REQ-042 A3, as locked);<br>4. at least one `kind: mini` with exactly three `solution` entries and `difficulty` 1 (REQ-045 statement and rule 3);<br>5. at least four `kind: warmup` of `difficulty` 1 (REQ-041 statement).<br>It prints the same five lines when it passes.<br>`app/build.gradle.kts` makes `:app:bundleRelease` depend on it: the AAB is the store artifact (Play takes app bundles). `:app:assembleRelease`, `:app:packageRelease` and every debug or test task never depend on it, so V-01, `ReleasePromiseKitTest` and `ReleaseBuildKitTest` keep their input at every step.<br>Validity (REQ-038) is not re-checked here: the release files are a subset of the debug bundle, which the content kit loads on every JVM line, and `ReleaseSetKitTest` loads the release set itself.<br>If `bundleRelease` turns out to need a signing configuration, that is a `recirculate → planner`, never a signing config (hard-stop 1) | REQ-042; REQ-045; REQ-041; REQ-039 rule 2; hard-stop 1 | the owner's AAB build (the checklist); TASK-065, TASK-070, TASK-071 | TASK-065 |
| S-57 | The launcher icon and the store icon (`tools/make_icon.py`, new; its test `tools/tests/test_make_icon.py`, new) | **Input:** DI-9, used as is: `Spec/icon/option-A-foreground.svg` (viewBox `0 0 108 108`; seven `path` elements, each with a `fill` and a `d` of absolute `M`, `L`, `Z` only) and `Spec/icon/option-A-background.txt` (`#FBF7EE`). Anything else in the SVG (another element, a `transform`, `text`, `image`, another path command) is refused with exit 1: the tool converts, it never redraws.<br>**Outputs** (UTF-8, LF; each XML opens with a comment naming the tool and DI-9):<br>- `app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_launcher_foreground.xml`: a `<vector>` of 108 dp × 108 dp, viewport 108 × 108, one `<path android:fillColor=… android:pathData=…/>` per SVG path, in order, the `d` and the `fill` copied verbatim;<br>- `app/src/main/res/values/ic_launcher_background.xml`: `<color name="ic_launcher_background">#FBF7EE</color>`;<br>- `app/src/main/res/mipmap-anydpi/ic_launcher.xml`: an `<adaptive-icon>` with exactly `<background android:drawable="@color/ic_launcher_background"/>` and `<foreground android:drawable="@drawable/ic_launcher_foreground"/>`. No `<monochrome>` layer (not asked; D3). No PNG mipmaps: at `minSdk 31` every device takes the adaptive icon;<br>- `release/pictures/icon-512.png`: 512 × 512, a 32-bit PNG (RGBA, colour type 6) with every alpha 255: opaque, the same pixels (`decisions.md` 2026-10-22 "Amends … (R7): the 512 × 512 store icon is a 32-bit PNG (RGBA, colour type 6) with every alpha 255 …"). The square shows the adaptive icon's visible window, x and y from 18 to 90 of the 108-unit viewBox, scaled by 512 / 72: the background colour everywhere, then the seven paths in order, each in its colour, the edges anti-aliased by a fixed supersampling. Play applies its own mask.<br>The tool is deterministic: two runs write the same bytes.<br>**The manifest** (S-58, TASK-067): `android:icon="@mipmap/ic_launcher"` on `<application>`; no `android:roundIcon` (D3; `minSdk 31`).<br>**The match** (REQ-048 A4): one source and one generator, so both icons show the same seven pieces on the same background. The kit checks it at points inside each piece and on the background: `LauncherIconKitTest` on the installed app, `StoreIconKitTest` on the files. The sample points are the author's, read from DI-9 and cited.<br>**No text:** DI-9 has none, the tool refuses a `text`, `tspan` or `image` element, and a vector holds only paths | REQ-048 rule 2 and A4 (CHG-002); DI-9; G-06, G-16 | the launcher; Play (the store icon); TASK-066's and TASK-069's legs | TASK-067 |

Density and ABI splits stay as AGP makes them: there is no density-specific or native resource.<br>- `<uses-feature android:name="android.hardware.screen.portrait" android:required="false" />` in the manifest. **The eleven questions, settled.** R-n is a planner reading for a `decisions.md` row.

1. **The release set (R1, R2, R4).** S-55 chooses it in `:content`, per build type: the release ships only the marked files, the debug build all 106, no cap (checkpoint item 1). S-56 is the release-readiness check; only `:app:bundleRelease` waits on it. With 0–19 marked: `assembleRelease` still builds (V-01's and `ReleasePromiseKitTest`'s input), its APK fails fast at start when empty and is never launched before the marks; `bundleRelease` refuses, naming what is missing; the REQ-042 legs are red with their counts. The tests read the release APK's files (R4): REQ-039 A2 under `play/` (`ReleaseMarksKitTest`), REQ-042 A1–A3 under `content/` (`ReleaseSetKitTest`, the release set loaded through `:content`'s loader). Fixtures: an unmarked puzzle (in situ, the unfiltered release APK before TASK-065, and crafted); 19 marked; three themes; no square; no three-piece mini; three warm-ups.
2. **The mark command (R3).** S-54, first (TASK-063). It prints the marked count by theme against REQ-042's bar, the square, the mini and the warm-ups, and `release-ready`. It unmarks. It changes one boolean, byte for byte. Stations run it on copies only.
3. **The store kit (R9, R10).** S-59: `release/` and the root `PRIVACY.md`. The mechanical self-check is TASK-069's untokened pin; REQ-001 A2 and REQ-048 A1–A2 stay the owner's look at the live listing.
4. **The launcher icon (R7).** S-57: an adaptive icon alone is enough at `minSdk 31`; no monochrome layer, no `roundIcon`; `android:icon` names it. The 512 store icon comes from the same tool and source. The device oracle is `LauncherIconKitTest` on three images; the file oracle is `StoreIconKitTest`.
5. **REQ-048 A3 on the installed app (R6).** The tokens go on `ReleasePromiseKitTest` (the release artifact's manifest) and `PlayThroughKitTest` (the installed app on every image). S-62 adds the platform's own view of the installed release package, as run-log evidence (TASK-067, TASK-070, TASK-071). It carries no token: it is a shell step, and its content equals the file check by construction. It is honest extra evidence, not a second oracle.
6. **REQ-002 A2 (R5).** S-63: `NoToddlerModeKitTest` under `play/` (the code home) and the settings half in `SettingsKitTest` under `app/`. The planted controls are a clickable "Toddler mode" text and an untagged clickable icon box.
7. **The store pictures (R11).** The icon (S-57) and the banner (S-60) are built before the marks. The screenshots (S-61) come after the marks, from the release APK, on the dev image (phone, at 1080 × 1920) and the tablet image. Sizes are checked by the pins and the script; the pictures are looked at. Checkpoint item 5.
8. **The release build's identity (R8).** S-58: `app.tangramplaytime` (checkpoint item 2); version 1 ("1.0.0"); the portrait feature not required; every language in the bundle's base; `localeFilters` untouched, so S-36 is not reopened.
9. **The other carries (R13).** The convex-13 credit line goes into `ASSET-LICENCES.md` (TASK-067). The licence line stays built and pinned untokened. No window is added (N3 stays a note). The D-pad sound is untouched.
10. **The owner's release checklist.** `release/CHECKLIST.md` (S-59), written by TASK-068. Its twelve steps are listed in S-59; the WO performs none of them.
11. **Coverage both ways and the channels.** The table below; the channels are in the intro and the briefs' table.

**Coverage both ways.** The code homes are `build-map.md` v1.10's: #Promise and #Release are system-level (`—`); #Solving and #PuzzleArt → `play/`; #PuzzleVolume inherits #Content → `content/`.
